#001
📋

Make one master list of every robot job — and check the owners actually do them

Details
  • List all the jobs in one place
  • Say who owns each job
  • Add a spot for a score and a cost
  • NEW: also write down what each job should produce, and when we last saw it produce it
  • Because three times now, a job moved to a new owner and quietly stopped getting done
  • Saying who owns it is not the same as checking they do it. That is the hole this fills
  • If a job has not produced anything for too long, that is a fault — same as any other
  • And right after a job changes hands, we watch it twice as closely for a while
  • Four new boxes and one check. Junyan's rule: keep it small, do not build a whole system
Technical notes

Build the Jobs registry in the operating library

Question

(Re-scoped 2026-07-15: canon moves to the owned local workspace store, not a Notion DB — Junyan's full-canon-off-Notion ruling, see data-sovereignty-wayfinder. Cloud runtimes read it via the pushed mirror; the store format/redaction is settled by the sibling's architecture ticket — coordinate before building, don't create new Notion canon.)

Stand up the Jobs registry in the owned store: a structured file set (or equivalent) where each desk job (J01–J29, T1 excluded) is a first-class entity with — at minimum — job id, name, one accountable owner, executing persona/runtime (relation), SOP link, cadence, scoreability, scorecard line (J17 placeholder), and cost line (J26 placeholder). Re-point persona pages to reference their job bundle rather than describe jobs inline, fixing the persona-vs-job conflation. Seed rows from the Job Board artifact. Confirm the schema with Junyan before creating (batched review, per his style); building is in-scope for this ticket.

J29 banked 2026-07-15 (session 2): Watchlist freshness — "the fast-track gate always reflects

who is verifiably booked in the space right now." Motivated by the emergency lane going live

(ticket 011): the Watchlist DB gates Path B and must stay fresh intraday (last-minute bookings).

Two lanes: (a) scheduled rebuild from the 5 calendars ∪ FBS board; (b) append-at-source —

whichever actor fast-tracks a same-day booking appends the row in the same action. End-state:

deterministic script writing desk-store/watchlist/YYYY-MM-DD.json (canon) + syncing the Notion

DB (the Zap's projection). Owner persona DELIBERATELY UNASSIGNED — Junyan: the Concierge is

too bloated to inherit it by default; the runtime assignment waits on the job-distribution view

(session-2 artifact) so the call is made against the whole load picture. (Note: ruling 4's "no

J29" meant no standing SkillOpt job — the number was never assigned; no conflict.)

Renderer-job scope note 2026-07-16 (Junyan-approved): register the published-board renderer as ONE job, not one per board — outputs: the watchlist build, the tabbed Day Sheet + desk-state snapshot (ticket 019), the Ops-lead FBS board (ticket 020), and the night-watch page including the absorbed Opener "Waiting on You" digest (007 ruling). One shared cadence (15-min GitHub Action), one failure mode, one cost line — that's the granularity the registry scores and bills at. ID collision to settle at schema review: J29 above is banked as Watchlist freshness, which is broader than rendering (its append-at-source lane is a write-path rule, not a render). Either broaden J29 to "published boards & watchlist freshness" (the rendering lane absorbs the boards; append-at-source stays a distinct rule inside it) or mint J30 for the renderer and keep J29 pure — Junyan picks at the batched schema review. Also at that review: name the renderer job's accountable owner (the Ops lead processes the Waiting-on-You queue on the morning shift, but owning "boards render correctly and on time" is a separate hat — and J29's persona is deliberately unassigned pending the job-distribution view), and settle a convention for minting new job IDs — the desk is still evolving past J28.

Handoff verification — REQUIRED schema, added 2026-08-05 (Junyan)

The registry must answer "is this job's owner actually doing it?", not just "who owns it?". Ruled

into this ticket rather than a separate effort — the registry is where it belongs, because the field

is the fix.

Why it's mandatory rather than nice-to-have. This map has now found the same failure three

times: the Vicky lockout (detection fired, nothing re-checked before she arrived), the dead

invariants checker (v2 written, never swapped in, stale page read as health for 5 days), and the PBF

sweep (050 — the job moved owner on 07-31 and stopped

being done the same day, for 5 days). The third is the one that proves the gap, because

everything upstream of it was done correctly: the handoff was deliberate, the owner was named per

035's standing lesson, the new owner's procedure was sound, and it carried two written tripwires —

JOB 1 Step 5's "still Unsent the next day means the sweep is broken" and the Responder's own pass

ledger. Neither was read. The signal existed and had no listener — the same disease as the

checker's stale page, one level up.

So the missing thing is not naming and not detection. It is that a handoff has no acceptance

test: no point in the process forces the question "is the new owner producing what the old owner

produced?". Three fixes were weighed (a written handoff checklist; one invariant per handoff; a

registry field). Junyan chose the registry — the checklist is one more rule needing a reader, which

is precisely what failed here, and a check per handoff grows the roster without bound.

Add to every job's schema:

  • - produces — the job's observable output, stated so a machine can look for it: a named

artifact, a DB row class, a queue row, a report line. A job whose output cannot be named this way

is a schema smell worth raising at the review, not a field to leave blank.

  • - last_verified_producing — timestamp of the last confirmed sighting of that output.
  • - verify_within — how long the job may go without producing before that silence is a defect.

Per-job, because cadences differ wildly (a 30-min sweep and a 10th/25th Accountant run are not

the same silence).

  • - owner_since — when the current owner took it. Makes a handoff a dated event the registry can

reason about, rather than a fact with no history.

The check that makes the fields real (otherwise this is just more unread prose): a job whose

last_verified_producing is older than its verify_within is a defect, escalating like any

other. It joins the invariant roster through 046's probation lane

like everything else — provisional first, human-marked real-or-noise, graduating on 5 clean days.

Sharper rule at a handoff: when owner_since changes, verify_within is halved for the first

two cycles. The PBF leak began on the first day under the new owner — that is exactly when a job

is least proven and most deserves a short leash.

Junyan's framing (2026-08-05): build it quickly, don't gold-plate it. Four fields and one

check. Do not let this grow into a handoff-workflow subsystem.

J31 minted provisionally 2026-07-21 — Claimed-shift mirror. Junyan asked for near-real-time

reflection of Open Shifts claims on Staff Scheduling, connected to a ticker as an operating-library

job. Built as library/job-31-claimed-shift-mirror.md (git canon) + Notion projection

(3a475032-81c4-813c-8ff8-f11f423d562a), wired as hourly step 6 on The Concierge. ID chosen to

sidestep the J30 renderer contest; confirm/renumber at the batched schema review. New capability

to bind at that review: calendar write (Staff Scheduling) — currently documented in the job

page only, not in CAPABILITIES; harnesses without it skip (Planner 19:30 backstops).

(shared keyword ≠ match; unsure → fallback); (b) step-22 Content-Type header — filter verified

working (0 Needs-review in test output; 14 Auto-send cards, ~5× smaller prompt). Defect filed

#053
🔁

Build the new way tickets get done — the robot suggests, Junyan says yes or fixes it

Details
  • A new list replaces the old 'things to do' board — one suggested next step per ticket
  • Each suggestion comes with the proof behind it, so Junyan can decide without digging
  • Two moves only: say yes, or rewrite the step to what it should have been, then mark it Ready
  • The robot only does steps marked Ready — never one Junyan hasn't seen
  • After doing it, the robot looks again at the real booking or calendar to check it truly happened
  • If Junyan already did the job himself, the robot spots it and ticks it off — no double work
  • Steps the robot gets right over and over become automatic; one rewrite and it goes back to asking
  • Trying it first on booking changes, the busiest and messiest kind
  • IN PROGRESS: the two new lists exist in Notion and the rulebook page is written
  • Checked what Skedda's robot arm can really do: move, create, cancel and approve bookings — but not change a price
  • Junyan ruled: the old to-do board gets finished off first, then retired — no copying rows over
  • Junyan ruled: instant bookings and moves run on their own, and he gets a text each time one happens
  • BUILT Aug 6: the little sweep that runs every 10 minutes — it checks the real booking for every open step and ticks off whatever already happened
  • It cannot touch bookings itself, on purpose: one robot does the work, a different one checks it. A checker that can also change things isn't a checker
  • Safety rule built in first: if it can't reach the booking system, it says 'couldn't check' — it never reads silence as 'the booking is gone'
  • Aug 15 — checked what was actually live: the little 10-minute sweep has been running since Aug 7 and works fine
  • But every single one of the 15 steps on the new list was written by a human. The robot had never suggested anything, not once
  • The thinking half simply had no robot to run it. The Concierge was supposed to be it, and the Concierge never gets that far — it runs out of time every pass
  • BUILT Aug 15: a new small robot, The Loop, that runs every hour and does the two missing parts — suggest the next step, and do the ones marked Ready
  • It is switched OFF until Junyan says go. One step waiting says 'text David Diep', and nobody ever approved that text — turning it on would send it
  • Three rules sit above everything in its instructions: only do steps marked Ready, never touch money, and never tick its own work off as done
  • Still to earn: a week of real use. The one thing to watch for is a step on the list that a robot wrote instead of a human
Technical notes

Build the Next Action queue + the advancement loop — pilot on Booking Changes

Question

Graduated from 018, which dissolved J01 and ruled that ticket

advancement is a loop, not a job. This ticket builds it and runs the pilot. It is the head of

the frontier — the new Concierge does not exist until this runs.

Build order matters: this does NOT wait on the Triager (053). Today's JOB 2

already creates Booking Changes tickets, so the loop pilots on real tickets from day one; the

Triager replaces the stub-making half afterwards.

1. The Next Action queue

A new Notion DB that retires ✅ Actions to Perform (Junyan: one action surface, not two —

plan the migration of open rows as part of this ticket, and see 018 §3).

One open proposal per ticket, max. Fields:

  • - Ticket relation, Artist relation
  • - Proposed action — the exact action text, editable by the reviewer. This is the field the

human rewrites.

  • - Basis — the evidence the agent read to propose it (the calendar read, the SOP step, the

thread history), so the reviewer can judge without re-investigating. A proposal whose Basis does

not let a reviewer decide is a defective proposal.

  • - Source SOP step relation
  • - Status — Proposed → Ready → Done, plus Won't Do. Reuse the Message Queue's verbs so staff

learn nothing new.

  • - Raised by, Action class, Promotion counter (per class, machine-readable — a field, never

prose)

  • - Executed by — agent or human (see §3)

2. The five beats

PROPOSE → REVIEW → EXECUTE → VERIFY → NEXT. The agent proposes ONE next action; the reviewer

either approves as-is or rewrites the action text, then marks it Ready; the agent executes

only Ready rows, never a Proposed one; then it re-reads the live source to confirm the

action actually happened and marks Done with the verification evidence. A read that disagrees is

raised as a mismatch — never marked Done. Then the next action is proposed, or the ticket

closes as resolved.

The rewrite is the feedback: the diff between proposed and rewritten text files an SOP improvement

against Source SOP step and resets that class's promotion counter to zero.

3. Executor-agnostic — the property that dissolves the back-capture tax

Because VERIFY reads ground truth, it does not care who acted. Junyan moving the booking in Skedda

himself, or sending the message himself, must close the row as Done / Executed by = human on

the next sweep with no capture work. A proposal overtaken by reality is closed, not re-proposed

and not nagged about. Build this as a first-class path, not an edge case — it is the single feature

that stops the system taxing Junyan for being faster than it.

Off-system actions no read can see keep the existing path: Slack #desk-drops.

4. Promotion ladder

Every action class starts human-gated. Approved-unmodified N consecutive times → auto-execute; any

rewrite → counter to zero. Money (charge / refund / credit / pricing), physical dispatch, and

alarm-code creation NEVER promote, whatever the counter reads. Decide N as part of the build

(open question — see the map).

Fast track from day one, no human turn: instant booking requests from Skedda and the platforms,

and booking moves (Junyan's ruling — time-critical and already ruled safe).

5. Tick

A thin sweep every 5–10 min: read the queue first, exit in seconds when nothing is Ready and

nothing waits; full agent pass only when there is real work. Cheaper than the current fixed cadence

that burns a pass whether or not there is anything to do. A true push path (event-gate fires the

agent on approval) is a later prototype, explicitly NOT a prerequisite.

6. Pilot

Booking Changes & Manual Help only. Everything else keeps running on today's rails.

Junyan reviews personally for week one — his corrections define the standard — then the Ops

Lead takes review. The Skedda write API makes this class genuinely executable end to end; verify

what it can actually write as step 0 (the broad canon correction is 058).

Acceptance

  • - A Booking Changes ticket walks end to end through the loop with every action seen before it

happened, and its Skedda write verified by read-back.

  • - A human-executed action closes its row automatically, with no back-capture by Junyan.
  • - At least one rewrite produces a recorded SOP improvement and a visible counter reset.
  • - Inherited from 051: no job at deferred-since >= 2 across 3

consecutive days, counters falling, intake still complete on a heavy day.

Interactive design walkthrough —

the Nadia Okonkwo booking move, four turns, with the rewrite beat and the promotion counter live.

Build log — 2026-08-05 (session 1 of 2; ticket stays OPEN)

Step 0 done — what Skedda can actually write. Verified against the live connector, recorded in

canon (library/the-loop.md, commit 3a573d45):

  • - edit_booking writes start, end, title, space_ids — and nothing else. Not price, not

payment status, not the holder.

  • - create_booking writes the full booking including price, payment_status and true repeating

series; cancel_booking deletes (whole series on a recurring id, refusing without

whole_series=True); release_occurrence frees one date; change_booking_holder reassigns;

approve_booking_request converts a request, with a price override.

  • - Read-back for every one of them: list_bookings / list_bookings_range /

list_booking_requests.

Two consequences that bind the design: a booking move is executable and verifiable end to end

(which is why it earns the pilot), and re-pricing an existing booking is not — so a move that

changes what is owed is two rows, the move (fast-tracked) and a money row (never promotes). Alarm.com

is still read-only.

Built:

  • - ⏭️ Next Action — collection://ddad960e-b850-4524-b04b-2522a21ac2f4

(page). Every field the ticket asked

for, plus three the beats turned out to require: Original proposal (the agent's verbatim

text, never edited — without it the rewrite diff is unrecoverable once the reviewer edits the

title in place), Verification + Verified at (Done demands evidence), and a

Mismatch status (beat 4 says a disagreeing read is never Done, so it needs somewhere to go).

Views: 👀 To Review · ▶️ Ready to Execute · ⚠️ Mismatches.

  • - 🎚️ Action Classes — collection://60333335-03f9-4ef7-80c2-5213c53b2b82

(page). The promotion counter is

per class, so it needed a per-class row, not a per-class field on a per-row table — the ticket's

"a field, never prose" is honoured by making the whole ladder data: Promotion counter,

Promotion N, Auto-execute, Never promotes, Fast track, Executor, Verify by. The loop

reads exactly one field to skip a human turn: Auto-execute. Ten classes seeded; money, physical

dispatch and alarm-code creation carry Never promotes.

  • - library/the-loop.md in canon — the five beats, the executor-agnostic close as a first-class

path, the ladder, the tick, the Skedda matrix, and the pass ledger.

Promotion N left blank on every class, deliberately — per the map's fog entry, it is set from

the pilot's own approve/rewrite ratios, not guessed in advance. A blank N never promotes.

Two questions raised and RULED same session (Junyan):

  1. 1. Retiring ✅ Actions to Perform. Live read found 86 open rows, only 35 ticket-linked — 51 are

system-health rows (28 Desk Invariants reviews, 11 Run Error, Custodian day-in-review) that

would break the one-open-proposal-per-ticket invariant if bulk-moved. Ruling: drain, then

deprecate — the open rows get processed to zero on the old board first, and only then does the

board retire. No bulk migration; the Next Action queue takes only new pilot-class work meanwhile.

  1. 2. Instant booking approval's side effect. approve_booking_request makes Skedda email the

requester their confirmation — an outbound with no human turn. Ruling: fast-track anyway, with

notify-after — every auto-executed fast-track action sends Junyan an SMS informing him it

happened. Auto-execute now ON for both fast-track classes. In canon (commit 36d96b5a).

Still to build (session 2): the tick runtime itself (venue: event-gate on Cloud Run, per the

043/042 precedent that GitHub Actions cron is disqualified for anything time-shaped) including the

notify-after SMS, the Actions-to-Perform drain, and then the pilot week — which is where every

acceptance criterion is actually earned.

Build log — 2026-08-06 (session 2; ticket stays OPEN)

The tick is built and green: 18 new tests, 212 across the whole gate, no regressions.

The architectural call — the tick is split in two, and the split is load-bearing.

event-gate on Cloud Run (POST /loop/<token>, kill switch LOOP_DISABLED) owns VERIFY and

REVIEW absorption; the agent pass keeps PROPOSE and EXECUTE. The line is judgment: the

deterministic half is a read and a comparison, so it is cheap enough to run every 10 minutes, and

it returns needs_agent_pass so the expensive half only wakes when a machine cannot settle the

question. An idle tick costs one Notion query and returns idle: true — that is the whole cost

argument over the fixed 2-hourly cadence it replaces.

The tick never writes to Skedda, by construction. Verification and execution are different

hands: one writer (the agent), one witness (the tick). A witness that can also write is not a

witness — so skedda_read.py is read-only, not merely used read-only.

The failure mode designed against first, because this map has already suffered it twice: a

failed read must never read as evidence. verify_booking returns unverifiable (not no-match)

when the Skedda read raises or the row carries no parseable Booking ref, and an unverifiable

row is left untouched. Without that, an expired session cookie returning "nothing is booked" would

flag or close every open row at once — the dead-checker pathology, one level up. Four of the

eighteen tests exist solely to hold this line.

Files: services/event-gate/loop.py (the tick), skedda_read.py (minimal read-only client —

cookie from SKEDDA_COOKIE or Secret Manager skedda-cookie, same project the service runs in),

test_loop.py, /loop/<token> in app.py. Canon updated (library/the-loop.md § Who runs which

beat) and RUNTIMES.md carries the new desk-loop job.

One convention this forced, now in canon: Booking ref is the machine-readable handle VERIFY

matches on and must be written <booking id> @ <YYYY-MM-DD> (e.g. 118625498 @ 2026-08-15). A

booking-class row without it can never close by itself — it is unverifiable forever.

Junyan's owner actions before the pilot can start (all need gcloud, historically permission-

blocked for Claude):

  1. 1. Deploy the revision: gcloud run deploy event-gate from services/event-gate (account

thedanceannex@ — the PERMISSION_DENIED in 036 was the wrong gcloud account, not permissions).

  1. 2. Grant the service access to the skedda-cookie secret, or set SKEDDA_COOKIE on the revision.
  2. 3. Create the desk-loop Cloud Scheduler job — every 10 min, 08:00–22:50 Toronto,

POST /loop/<GATE_PATH_TOKEN>.

  1. 4. Smoke-test with ?dry_run=true first: it reports what it would close and writes nothing.

Still to build (session 3): the agent pass (PROPOSE/EXECUTE against Ready rows), the

notify-after SMS wired to real fast-track executions (notify_fast_track exists but is unwired —

nothing auto-executes yet, so nothing can fire it), the Actions-to-Perform drain, and the pilot

week.

what blessed rows imply:

fields complete) → compute due messages from arrival/departure times (pure date math) → merge

the canned template verbatim (placeholder fill only, no composition) → write the 📬 Zapier

write-order gotcha) → hard dedup: one queue row per booking × message type, checked before

under-admit, never guess.

change-only (queries the studio's latest row first), Toronto observation time, never

Technical notes

Design the Sweep-Feedback decision-capture loop

Question

The 🔧 Sweep Feedback inbox accumulated ~27 unactioned New rows over three weeks because

the loop has no closing step: J0/J15 file the signal and the desk applies quick fixes, but

the "human accepts → land in canon → mark Applied" step for design / HTR / owner-directive rows

has no owner and no cadence, so rows sit New indefinitely. Design the standing loop that

prevents re-accumulation. Settle:

  • - Routing by row type (the exits, so nothing stays New):
  • - HTR proposals/suggestions → land as Needs review rows in the How-to-Respond DB,

sourcing wording from the actual sent message (now queryable via the J28 ledger — no more

archaeology), then mark the sweep row Applied with a link. (Pattern proven 2026-07-17.)

  • - Owner directives → straight to capture-sop-update (pre-approved by definition).
  • - Design sessions → exactly two exits: graduate to this wayfinder map, or park with a

one-line "won't do." Never left New.

  • - Attributed defects (the new J13/JOB 7 stream, ticket 008) → the critic/optimizer feed;

define whether/when they get a human pass or stay machine-consumed.

  • - Owner + cadence: who runs the accept pass, and on what beat — candidate: a weekly ~20-min

clear riding the Monday-AM meeting that ticket 004 already scheduled.

  • - Relation to J0/J16: this is the operational form of the feedback-apply jobs, which exist but

don't close the loop. Decide whether this is J16's spec or a distinct job — coordinate with the

registry (001) and the J17/SkillOpt lane (sibling map) so the accept pass and the scorecard/

optimizer reads don't fork.

Success = a documented, owned, cadenced loop such that the Sweep Feedback board is cleared on a

regular beat and never accumulates a multi-week backlog again.

Context

Graduated 2026-07-17 from the Sweep-Feedback processing session (27 → 15 open). The processing

itself proved the routing (created 3 HTR rows + edited 1, closed 6 superseded/resolved, recorded

4 owner-approved-pending) — this ticket makes that a standing process instead of a one-off cleanup.

Technical notes

Dissolve the Receptionist — quick answers become watchlist-verified canned sends on the J03 event lane

Question

Ruled by Junyan 2026-07-21 (grilled in the 025 step-2 session): the Receptionist persona

dissolves. The watchlist quick answers stop being a polling LLM job and become

event-driven canned sends on the same infrastructure as the J03 same-day emergency

replies — Phone.com inbound webhook → Zap → watchlist-verified → auto-send in seconds.

No LLM in the hot path. First concrete piece of the "immediate-support lane" fog (022).

Rulings already made (don't re-litigate):

  • - Canned responses only (HTR rows), set up the same way as the emergency lane.

Watchlist-verified. Unmatched / unverified inbounds fall through silently to the

normal held-reply flow (Concierge) — the lane never guesses.

  • - "Code request" and "code complaint" are the SAME case: send them their code.

"What's my code" and "my code isn't working" both get the code card (a re-entry

usually fixes it); genuine emergencies already have the life-safety path. No

complaint-vs-request branching.

  • - Codes are canned + lookup: the card is templated, the fill (their code / their

window / today's studio) is a deterministic read — the watchlist row is the natural

carrier (J29's watchlist_build.py may need to add the code/window/studio fields;

check what each card needs: sound, hot/cold, lockbox are static per studio).

  • - The HTR review process gains a standing classification step: every HTR

suggestion/row reviewed is ALSO judged "deterministic-eligible → promote to the

fast-track (event-lane) card list" vs "needs judgment → stays with the Concierge."

The canned lane grows through the existing HTR governance loop, not ad-hoc edits.

(Touches the HTR review flow in JOB 0 / process-sweep-feedback — the HTR carve-out

#037
📰

Make sure the robot always reads its morning reports

Details
  • On Jul 28 the main robot skipped reading the overnight reports — it ran out of room
  • The night checker caught it and raised a red flag (that part worked)
  • Two fixes went in the same day: read reports FIRST, and a broken file link was repaired
  • Now we watch 3 mornings: if the flag stays green, we're done
  • If not: a small program will squeeze all the reports into one short page every night
  • That way the morning read is always small enough to finish
Technical notes

Make morning report intake constant-size — verify the reorder, build the night digest only if needed

Question

On the 2026-07-28 morning pass the Concierge skipped report intake entirely — it ran out of context after doing its desk jobs, and the Night Watch flagged a red "unverified — treat as not all-clear" banner. Source handoff: HANDOFF-report-intake-too-big.md. Structural risk: one pass carries all desk jobs + full report intake, and intake is what gets sacrificed when the context budget runs out — silently, until an external auditor notices.

Two mitigations already landed the same day: (1) the routine reordered so intake fires FIRST on the morning pass; (2) the 6-day git-canon outage (the biggest context drain — every pass burned tokens on Notion fallbacks) was fixed at 10:18 EDT. So this ticket is gated: verify before building.

Phase 1 — verify (do not build yet). Watch the acceptance window: 3 consecutive mornings (starting with the 2026-07-28 4:10 PM run as the first signal) with report intake completed + all desk jobs done + no red Night Watch banner for "reports not consumed". If clean, close this ticket as "reorder + git fix sufficed" and bank the digest as a known design.

Phase 2 — build the pre-digest (only if a morning stays red, or a heavy-report day overflows again). Make intake small and constant-size:

  • - night_digest_build.py in desk-correspondence/scripts/ (pattern: day_sheet_build.py, ticket 019's deterministic no-persona lane): collect the overnight Workflow Reports, boil to ONE bounded markdown digest (reports/night-digest-YYYY-MM-DD.md, newest-first; per report: status line, failures/degradations, human-items, pointer to full report).
  • - Scheduling correction to the source handoff: NOT the Mac scheduler — the local lane was disabled by the Brazil ruling (ticket 031, "move everything to the cloud"; Mac = fallback only). Ride the cloud deterministic lane (the 30-min watchlist Action or a dedicated job), and because it is morning-critical, back it with the Apps Script tick pattern (the scheduler-reliability fog: bare GitHub cron drops runs) so it reliably finishes before the first Concierge fire (08:10 Toronto).
  • - Repoint the Concierge instructions: morning intake = read the digest (git-first), open a full report only when flagged; digest read stays the first job of the pass.
  • - Repoint the Night Watch audit so digest-based intake counts as consumption — coordinate with the Custodian night pass (ticket 027), which owns that audit surface.

Out of scope / fallback: the "Report Reader" split routine (handoff step 3) — do not build pre-emptively; if the digest itself isn't enough, that's a new ticket. Don't duplicate the same-day notion_log_ingest.py SKIP_MEDIUMS fix (separate background task).

Resolution records: the 3-morning verification result; if built — script location, schedule + tick, the Concierge + Night Watch repoints, and a heavy-day test.

Sibling note: today's incident is logged on self-improving-desk-wayfinder as evidence for its thesis (a monolith pass silently sacrifices jobs under context pressure) — no build lands there.

Details
  • Today, when the sending program says 'Sent', we just believe it
  • But phone records and the email Sent folder show what REALLY left the building
  • New plan: compare every 'Sent' message against those real records
  • If a message claims 'Sent' but never shows up there, raise a flag — never stay quiet
  • Even a clean day gets reported, so silence can't hide a breakdown
Technical notes

Send verification — prove a dispatched message actually went out

Question

Raised by Junyan 2026-07-31: "I want a way to double-check to see if the message we

sent actually went out." Today the desk's proof of sending is the send engine (Apps

Script poller, repair 029) flipping a 📤 Message

Queue row to Status = Sent — a self-report, not ground truth. If the Phone.com or

Gmail API call silently failed after the flip (or the poller died mid-batch), nothing

would notice; the row would read as delivered forever.

Build the send-verification loop, deterministic and no-persona:

  • - Ground truth is the J28 ledger — the SMS poller reads Phone.com's sent messages

and gmail_pull.py reads the Sent mailbox, so a genuinely dispatched message appears

in the ledger within ~30 min regardless of what the queue row claims.

  • - The check: for every queue row flipped to Sent, confirm a matching ledger

outbound exists (recipient + timestamp window + body match) within a grace window.

A Sent row with no ledger counterpart files a Run Error / Attributed defect row

(Causing Job = send engine) — never a silent pass. Report zero-mismatch results too

(the 035 lesson: a failure that looks like a quiet success).

  • - Where it runs: likely a small script on the 30-min watchlist Action (same rails as

the pollers), or a leg of day_sheet_build.py — decide in-build; no new agent.

  • - Seam with 015: 015's remaining item 1 is the

drafted-vs-sent diff (queue body vs actual-sent text — content fidelity). This

ticket is the existence check (did it go out at all). Same join (queue row ↔ ledger

outbound); build the join once so 015's diff can ride it — coordinate, don't

double-build. Also the sanctioned send-time Message Body snapshot (sibling 004's

ground-truth note) lives on this same seam.

  • - Surface: mismatches should land where Junyan already looks — the Fixer Report /

night watch "Fix these" feed, not a new page.

Resolution records: the checker (where it runs, cadence, match rules), the first live

mismatch-or-clean-week readout, and the 015 seam note (what 015 still owns after this).

Technical notes

Build the deadline escalation ladder in event-gate — no booking starts behind an open access flag

Question

Build the design ruled in 035 (2026-08-03). Everything below is

settled — this ticket is construction, not design. Re-open 035's rulings only if the build proves

one of them impossible.

The invariant: no booking may start while an Access / PIN flag against it is still open.

Re-verify against the live panel (alarm-mcp), never the Notion row status. A passing check

auto-closes the flag silently — nobody is touched when the system heals itself.

Home: the existing event-gate Cloud Run service, on its 1-min Cloud Scheduler tick.

Not a GitHub Action (Actions cron silently drops runs — fatal for a deadline check), not the Mac,

not a new service.

The four rungs

| Rung | Trigger | Action | Owner |

|---|---|---|---|

| 1 | Morning of the booking | Heads-up to the morning ops person (Ela) | The Doorman, folded into its existing ~06:00 access look |

| 2 | Continuous | Re-verify every open access flag against the live panel every 30 min; silent while unresolved-but-not-urgent | event-gate |

| 3 | 2h before booking start, still failing | Direct message to Junyan — a real push (escalation.py path), not a queue row | event-gate |

| 4 | 45 min before start, still failing | Send the Alarm.com Emergency Code user to the renter, and file the mandatory reset row | event-gate |

Rung 4 — handle as a master key, not a PIN

The Alarm.com Emergency Code user is Locations: All / Access Period: At All Times /

Access Type: Existing + Future — every studio, no expiry, including studios not yet created.

Each send permanently arms one more person with entry to everything until a human rotates it.

Junyan accepted this trade because alarm-mcp is read-only by deliberate design

(adc_client.py: "exposes NO arm / disarm / lock / unlock calls") — there is no way to mint a

time-boxed per-renter user the way Junyan did by hand on Jul 24, so the standing code is the only

unattended path through a door.

Never write the code digits into git, a ticket, a log line, or a Notion body. Reference the

Alarm.com user by name; read the value at send time from a secret.

Mandatory rotation guardrail — the send is not complete until all three land:

  1. 1. A row in ✅ Actions to Perform: emergency code must be reset. Records recipient, time,

studio, booking. Urgent-until-confirmed — this row must not be allowed to age quietly.

  1. 2. A loud notice to both Junyan and Ela that the key went out and to whom.
  2. 3. A J28 ledger entry for the send.

Build order

All four rungs ship together, sequenced 1→4 (Junyan, 2026-08-03) — explicitly not 1–3 first, so

the ladder cannot end up permanently three-quarters built.

Acceptance test — replay Jul 24 (Vicky Yeung, 509A, 19:30)

Flag raised 06:02. Expected: Ela told ~06:02 · re-verified every 30 min all day ·

Junyan paged 17:30 · at 18:45, code sent + reset row filed. Zero silent hours.

Also required (035's cross-cutting rule, ticket 036 lineage)

Each rung reports its own result every pass including zero, and files a Type = Run Error row

if it cannot run. A failure that looks like a quiet success is how the original 13.5 hours hid.

Banked risk to audit

If the reset rows are filed and then ignored, the guardrail becomes the very ignored-queue-row

disease this ticket cures — one level up. First post-launch audit: are reset rows being cleared?

#046

Build the probation desk for new safety checks

Details
  • New checks are not trusted on day one — this builds the waiting room they sit in
  • A check on probation writes its findings quietly and bothers nobody
  • Each morning a person reads them and marks each one real or noise
  • Five clean days and the check graduates. One noise mark and the count goes back to zero
  • Must be built before any of the new checks are switched on
  • Also answered here: the thank-you-message backlog. Old one is clear — but a NEW one started Jul 31
  • That is the exact day thank-you messages changed hands. Seven are still unsent
Technical notes

Build the invariant probation lane — provisional findings, human review, graduation

Question

Graduated from 044, which ruled that all three families of

bookend checks ship together behind a human review lane rather than staggered. This ticket

builds the lane. It blocks both check-builds — no new invariant lands before the lane exists,

because the lane is the thing that keeps a wrong check from crying wolf.

Build in dc-canon/services/event-gate/invariants.py plus the Custodian's 09:35 pass:

  • - Maturity on every check — Provisional or Escalating. A provisional check runs on the

normal :25 pass but writes only to a Provisional block on the 🩺 Desk Invariants page: no ✅

Actions row, no page, and it does not feed the 3-runs-in-a-row escalation counter.

  • - Review surface — the Custodian's 09:35 pass presents each provisional finding for a real /

noise mark. Decide the mechanics: where the mark is stored, and how the 09:35 pass renders a

day's provisional findings without becoming a wall (043 capped escalations at 5 rows/run for

exactly this reason — the review list needs its own answer).

  • - Graduation — Escalating after 5 consecutive review days with zero noise marks; any noise

mark resets to zero. Graduation must be a recorded event, not a silent flip.

  • - Family policy — existence and completeness checks graduate; reconciliation checks never do

(044's ruling: a two-list diff is a judgment call permanently). The lane needs to express

"provisional forever" as a first-class state, not as a check that simply never reaches 5.

  • - Inherited invariant: no check counts its own output as desk state — the Raised by == SELF

exclusion that 045 had to retrofit onto B and C must be structural here, not per-check.

State lives in the chunked JSON code blocks on the 🩺 page (043's pattern — Cloud Run is ephemeral,

and the unchunked version silently overran Notion's 2000-char cap).

044 carried "has JOB 33 been clearing the PBF backlog since Jul 22?". Live read of the 🛎️ FBS AI

Support board (collection://36475032-81c4-80d6-b18a-000b8d6f9421) says JOB 33 never cleared it —

it stopped owning it. PBF initial sends moved off JOB 33 to The Responder on 2026-07-31

(Junyan's ruling; library/the-responder.md Scope + pass step 7, 30-min cadence, "it's not a

crucial step"). JOB 1 Step 5 is a retirement stub pointing there.

| Booking date | Booking | Studio | Type |

|---|---|---|---|

| 2026-08-03 | Marco Alberto | 901 | Monitor Only |

| 2026-08-03 | Kristel San Jose | 901 | FBS |

Read the dates. The Jul-22-era backlog is clear — every completed booking from Jun 28 through

Nobody read it.

This is the exact pathology the whole invariant effort exists to catch, one more time: a

retirement handed a job to a new owner, nobody checked the new owner was doing it, and the desk's

own written tripwire went unread for five days. 035's standing lesson — *a retirement is not done

until every step of the retired job names its new owner* — was honoured here; what was missing is

the step after it, that naming an owner is not the same as verifying the owner acts.

Two pieces of work fall out, neither owned by this ticket:

Decide who presses send on a thank-you message

Details
  • A thank-you dies if nobody releases it within one day
  • Right now it is shown to a person once a night and never again
  • Choice 1: let the robot just send it, like it already does for the we-missed-you text
  • Choice 2: keep the check, but nag before the day runs out

  • Junyan's call. Worth noting: we already auto-send to people who did NOT show up
Technical notes

A held PBF has 24h to be released or it dies — who releases it, and what happens when nobody does

Question

Graduated from 050, which found that the Responder's PBF

sweep works: it finds every completed booking on its own day and queues the PBF within the hour.

Seven PBFs still died between 2026-07-31 and 2026-08-04 — every one of them sitting at

Pending Review, correctly drafted, never released.

Two canon rules meet here and, together, guarantee losses:

  • - Every PBF is held — the-responder.md Scope and AUTONOMY: nothing this lane composes leaves

Pending Review, and a human releases it.

  • - A PBF more than 1 day past its booking is never sent (Junyan, 2026-08-05).

So a held PBF has ≤24h of shelf life, and the only thing that surfaces it is the courtesy-reply

approval block on the Responder's 22:50 pass. One appearance, one night. Miss that night and the

message is dead — and no later pass re-surfaces it, because the cross-status dedup (correctly) sees

a row already exists and reports found · deduped forever.

Decide the release policy. The candidates, with what each costs:

  1. 1. Auto-send the PBF, as the no-show "we missed you" text already does. The precedent exists and

the confidence gate is the same one: Booking Status = Complete, set upstream by Concierge JOB 1

Step 4, which the desk already trusts to auto-send on. Cheapest, and it removes the human from a

message class with no per-case judgment in it. Cost: a wrong Complete flip now reaches a

customer unreviewed.

  1. 2. Keep the hold, add a deadline escalation — an unreleased PBF pages before its window closes

rather than after. Rides 042's ladder. Cost: another thing

to read, which is precisely what failed here; and it still needs a human awake inside 24h.

  1. 3. Re-surface every pass, not once a night. Cheapest change to the current shape, but it turns

the approval block into a repeating list — and the 2026-08-03 ping-pong lesson says a line

repeated on consecutive passes is a dropped ball wearing a uniform, not a fix.

Junyan's call, and it is the shape of the fix, not a detail of it — 050 established the

mechanism, so this ticket is the ruling and the canon edit that follows it, nothing more.

Note the asymmetry worth putting to him directly: the no-show text — sent to someone who did not

show up — auto-sends today, while the thank-you to someone who did is held. If the risk model

justifies the first, it is hard to argue it forbids the second.

Related but not this ticket: 049's check U detects an unresolved

PBF after the fact. Detection is settled; this is about preventing the loss in the first place.

Find a write path for Alarm.com codes — a lockout is worse than the write risk

Question

Graduated from 018. PIN generation is human-only across every code SOP

because alarm-mcp is read-only by design, which makes every access-code issue park on a staff

gate. Junyan named the risk asymmetry plainly: *"Alarm.com does not have a write API, but I want to

find a solution around this as it's not a high risk to write in the code. It's high risk if someone

is not able to get in."*

The Vicky Yeung lockout (035) is the standing evidence: detection

worked, and a renter still could not get in. 042's rung 4 —

sending the Alarm.com Emergency Code — exists only because the safer per-renter, time-boxed user is

  • - what Alarm.com actually exposes — documented API, partner/dealer API, or nothing
  • - browser automation from event-gate as the fallback (the Codex in-app-browser precedent from

  • - credential handling and blast radius — write access to the alarm system is the most dangerous

capability on the desk; propose the containment before proposing the path

This ticket is built on two claims that have never been probed at the wire: *"Alarm.com has no

descriptions, and 063 found that class of claim false or overstated 6 times out of 7. Canon now

designing any workaround — the workaround may not be needed, and if it is, the probe is what

proves it. Record the evidence either way; that closes two rows of 065.

#060
🏓

Messages nobody claims — make 'not my job' leave a mark

Details
  • Three renters this week wrote to us and no robot picked it up — Junyan caught each one himself
  • Each robot looked, decided 'not mine', and wrote nothing down — so it looked like nothing was wrong
  • The rule going in: every message must end somewhere — handled, given a ticket, or a robot's name on the 'no'
  • A separate watcher, outside all the robots, rings when a message sits too long with no answer
  • It names the person waiting — 'Himanshi, booking question, 4 hours' — not just a number

  • Case four, same day: Francisco texted about lost AirPods at 1pm — the collector delivered it 5 hours late, then no robot picked it up either
  • Lesson for the watcher: count from when the renter sent it, not from when our copy arrived
Technical notes

Ping-pong — every inbound message gets a recorded disposition, checked from outside the passes

Question

From HANDOFF-concierge-ping-pong.md (2026-08-06,

diagnosed not fixed). A renter's message lands in the Correspondence Log; the Responder decides

it is not its lane and leaves it; the Concierge never picks it up; no error, no skip record, no

ticket — the message just ages. Three cases in one week (Shauna Hardy, Himanshi Mehta across two

consecutive Responder passes, David Diep's follow-through), all resolved by Junyan, none by the

fleet. Junyan's framing ruling: *picking up new messages in the Correspondence Log is part of the

Concierge's sweep — and that part is not happening.*

Case 4, traced live 2026-08-06 (same day the handoff was written): Francisco Delgado texted at

17:02Z — lost-and-found, AirPods + lipstick, offering to return them Wednesday. The message

reached the git ledger only at 21:55Z (~5h late — the 062 feed gap, observed in the wild), and

then no robot claimed it: the only CC ticket created all afternoon was Tufan Bhattarai's

booking move (which the desk handled well — ticketed in ~1.6h, Skedda writes verified by re-read).

7+ hours in: no ticket, no reply, no disposition. The two defects compound — a feed gap followed

by a no-claim is indistinguishable from a lost message, and only a human reading raw SMS catches

it. Note for the detector design: it must clock from the message's own timestamp, not its

ledger arrival, or the 062 gap eats the detection window.

The core defect: declining to act is invisible. Every pass reports success because, from each

agent's view, nothing went wrong. Nothing in the fleet records what a job chose not to do.

Prompt-level fixes have failed three times (pass ledger skipped on 3 of 5 passes; 3-deferral

escalation unpaid next morning; claimed-but-undispatched message invisible 13 days). Common

property: the detector lived inside the failing pass. The handoff's conclusion, adopted as this

ticket's premise: the check must live outside the passes and require no agent's cooperation.

Two halves to resolve:

  1. 1. Ground truth first (AFK, before the grilling): read actual Concierge pass transcripts and

answer §4's candidate break points — does the sweep issue a Correspondence Log query at all?

Is there a watermark? Do the drops correlate with long passes (the 051 starvation shape)?

§4's points 1–2 and 5 produce identical symptoms and need different fixes; do not guess.

  1. 2. The design decision (Junyan's, per the handoff): the disposition contract + the external

detector. Candidate shape to grill, not to assume: every Correspondence Log entry must reach a

recorded disposition (actioned / linked to ticket / explicitly declined, decliner named), and a

new invariant — *no log entry older than N without a disposition and without a linked

ticket* — rides the 046 probation lane like any other check, named per message ("Himanshi

Mehta, Aug-15 booking request, 4h, no disposition"), immune to pass truncation.

Seams that bound the design — settle them in the grilling, don't rediscover them later:

  • - The Triager (054) is the structural fix for intake — its whole job is

"read inbound, make the stub, stop." Decide whether this ticket ships an interim detector and

054 ships the disposition writer, or 054 accelerates and this ticket is detection only. Do NOT

build a second intake path here.

  • - The invariant venue exists — event-gate's checker + the 046 probation lane. A new check

goes there, not into any persona's prompt (the three failed attempts all lived in prompts).

  • - Detection without repair makes the pile bigger: JOB 7 — the repair path — did not run at

all on Aug 5 while detection ran ~15 times (§6). The acceptance below includes the repair path

actually draining what the detector finds.

  • - The store is corrupted and the feed is flaky — duplicates (061)

and the watchlist-refresh failure that keeps SMS turns out of the ledger

(062). Any watermark/disposition scheme inherits both;

build order may need 061 first.

Acceptance

From the handoff's §7, adopted verbatim as the fixed-when:

  1. 1. Every Correspondence Log entry reaches a recorded disposition; silence is not a disposition.
  2. 2. An entry with no disposition past a bounded interval raises a signal from outside the

passes — no agent cooperation required.

  1. 3. The signal names the message and the renter, not a count.
  2. 4. It survives a truncated pass — fires exactly when load is highest.

Plus: the three known cases would each have been caught within N, and the detector's findings are

demonstrably drained (repair path alive), not accumulating.

#061
👯

The message log keeps writing the same message twice — fix the copier

Details
  • 18 batches of doubled messages in one day; it's the copying program, not the robots
  • Why it matters: every robot checks this log to avoid doing things twice — a messy log fools them all
  • Fix the copier, clean out the doubles, and add a check so it stays clean

Technical notes

The Correspondence Log poller writes duplicates — fix the writer, dedupe the store

Question

From HANDOFF-concierge-ping-pong.md §6: the

Correspondence Log (collection://defec0fd-e817-4191-8a50-b69ad3e72b59) is accumulating

duplicates — 18 duplicate groups in 24 hours across three consecutive windows, 5 open feedback

rows — and the evidence points at the poller, not agent behaviour.

Why it outranks its size: this log is the shared dedup store every robot reads, and it is the

same store the ping-pong sweep (060) fails to read. Any

watermark or per-message disposition scheme 060 designs inherits these duplicates — a

disposition keyed to one copy of a message leaves its twin looking unhandled, which would

re-manufacture ping-pong inside the fix. Likely 060's build depends on this landing first; 060's

grilling confirms.

Scope:

  1. 1. Reproduce: pull the 18 groups, establish the duplicate key (same message id? same

body+timestamp with different ids?) and which poller writes them (the two-way Notion sync

cursors from the 07-18 design are a suspect — check both directions).

  1. 2. Fix the writer so it is idempotent against the observed key.
  2. 3. Clean the store: merge/close existing duplicate rows so downstream dedup reads are trustworthy.
  3. 4. Add the check that keeps it fixed: a duplicate-group count on the invariant roster (probation

lane per 044's standing rule — a day of live data before it may escalate).

Acceptance

  • - Zero new duplicate groups across 48h of live polling.
  • - Existing duplicates merged or closed; the 5 feedback rows resolved with the root cause named.
  • - The invariant watches it (Provisional is fine).

#067
🗂️

Customer mail that lands in spam never reaches canon — even after a human rescues it

Details
Technical notes

Customer mail that lands in spam never reaches canon — even after a human rescues it

Question

Found while checking 062's backfill. Camille Quesada emailed the desk 2026-08-06 19:32Z

("Booking for Sunday, August 9th" — asking for door codes and alarm instructions). Junyan's

Aug-7 reply reached canon; her original never did, so the thread read as the desk writing

unprompted. Every other turn across Aug 5–7 was present — SMS matched 200/200 against Phone.com,

and 14 of 15 checked Gmail turns were in the ledger. This was the only hole, and it was not

caused by the outage: the poller's cursor rides in the same commit as the messages, so an

outage-lost message re-captures on the next tick. This one was skipped, not lost.

Most likely mechanism (not yet proven): the message was in spam when the poller ran.

gmail_pull.py lists with includeSpamTrash: false and only ever looks forward from its cursor

(after:{cursor - 3600}). So a message quarantined at poll time is invisible, and staying

invisible is permanent — a human un-spamming it later changes nothing, because the cursor has

already moved past that hour. Her thread carries no labelIds at all, consistent with a

message that was rescued from spam rather than filed by a normal Gmail filter.

Backfilled by hand: messages/2026/08/06/gmail-19fd88ffa03e12ca.json (commit c80c0f99,

source: backfill-gmail-poller-gap), and the thread now renders correctly.

Decide the fix:

  1. 1. Confirm the mechanism first. Is spam the actual cause? Check whether Gmail still shows

any spam-history signal for that message, and test the poller against a deliberately

spam-filed message before designing around the theory.

  1. 2. Then choose the repair. Options seen so far: poll with includeSpamTrash: true and drop

true spam by rule in code (the ledger already enforces ledger_exclude.json that way); or

add a slower, wider re-scan pass (e.g. once a day over the last 7 days, dedup by filename)

so any forward-cursor skip self-heals rather than becoming permanent.

  1. 3. Consider the class, not just the cause. A cursor that only moves forward turns every

kind of skip into a permanent hole. The re-scan option fixes the class; the spam flag fixes

one instance.

Acceptance

  • - Mechanism confirmed or refuted with a real test, not inference.
  • - A message the poller skipped on its first pass demonstrably reaches canon on a later one.

J31 starves at the tail of the pass, and nothing re-reads an AVA after the Host builds it

Question

its own runtime.

Defect B — an AVA is computed once and never revisited. The Host builds every AVA at

A missing MIRRORS row takes the whole ledger pipeline down — the canon check is a full-run gate

Details
Technical notes

A missing MIRRORS row takes the whole ledger pipeline down — the canon check is a full-run gate

Question

Graduated from 066. Twice now, a content gap in canon has

failed an entire watchlist-refresh run:

  • - 2026-08-06 — library/the-loop.md had no MIRRORS row (found by 062, fixed 136896ba).
  • - 2026-08-12 — library/job-35-messaging-triage.md had no MIRRORS row; four runs red across an hour.

The mechanism is structural, not accidental. Canon completeness check runs as a step inside

the same job as the ledger pollers, so exit 1 on a documentation bookkeeping problem marks

the run failed — and while the pollers themselves ran before it and their pushes landed, the

run's red status is what a human or a monitor reads. A missing MIRRORS row is a real defect

worth surfacing, but it is not a reason to report "the ledger tick failed".

Decide the shape:

  1. 1. Should the canon check gate the run at all, or report separately (its own workflow, its

own Run Error key, its own cadence)? The J28 pipeline's health and canon's projection

completeness are different questions with different owners.

  1. 2. If it stays in the run, should it be non-blocking (continue-on-error, file its own

distinct Run Error row) so a doc gap never masquerades as a poller failure?

  1. 3. Prevention: a new library/*.md with no MIRRORS row is a predictable authoring slip.

Should the mapping be enforced at write time (a pre-commit check, or the mirror row created

with the file) rather than caught hours later by a red tick?

Related, and worth settling in the same conversation: a re-run overwrites a run's visible

conclusion. Run 31858026100 shows success in gh run list while attempt 1 failed and

fired the Run Error handler. Anything that audits fleet health by reading conclusion — a

future invariant, a monitor, a sweep — will undercount failures unless it reads run_attempt

or the attempts endpoint. Name the rule so it lands in canon, not just in a ticket.

Acceptance

  • - A ruling on whether the canon check blocks the run, and where its failures get reported.
  • - A named prevention point for missing MIRRORS rows (write-time vs run-time).
  • - The re-run/conclusion measurement rule written into canon so audits stop undercounting.

#039

Always know WHO sent every customer message

needs 038 first
Details
  • On Jul 30, eleven held messages went out in one hour — and no record said who did it
  • It was Ela doing her job, but a runaway program would have looked exactly the same
  • The sending robot stamps its own name over the person who pressed go
  • Fix: the robot notes who handed it each message BEFORE stamping, and AI helpers announce a send before making it
  • Then a checker matches every real sent message to a known sender — anything unmatched raises a flag
Technical notes

Send attribution — every outbound must match one authorised origin

Question

Raised on the 2026-07-31 fixer run, from two live incidents:

  1. 1. The Jul 30 bulk release — 11 held 📤 Message Queue rows (several carrying

explicit DO-NOT-RELEASE prose) flipped to Sent in one hour. It took a day and a

direct question to Junyan to learn it was Ela working the queue, because Notion

keeps only last_edited_by and the send engine overwrites the releaser's identity

seconds after every release (created_by is also useless — every agent writes

through Junyan's integration token). A staff member clearing her list and a

runaway program are indistinguishable in the record.

  1. 2. The direct-send hole — an LLM running process-studio-tickets (or any skill with

send_sms) can put a message on the wire with no queue row at all. The J28 ledger

records it as source: api-sms-poll — capture method, not actor. Phone.com's raw

API confirms (checked 2026-07-31): no user/device/client attribution exists at the

carrier; everything sends as ext 100 "Dance Annex".

The design (ruled directions, 2026-07-31 fixer session)

Reconcile, don't enumerate. The invariant: *every outbound message in the J28

ledger matches exactly one authorised origin.* Three parts:

  • - Release capture (send-engine hook, NOT a poller). Junyan rejected the 3-min

poller as too much machinery. Instead the Apps Script send engine (repair 029)

reads the row's last_edited_by BEFORE it claims/overwrites the row — at that

moment it is still the releaser — and writes the actor into its send record.

Event-driven, zero polling, cannot miss a send.

  • - Send-intent declarations (direct-send lane). Any skill/agent authorised to send

off-queue writes a one-line intent to the ledger (who, recipient, body hash, ticket)

BEFORE calling send_sms/Gmail. An undeclared send must never look authorised.

  • - The reconciler. Rides 038's queue-row ↔ ledger-outbound join (build the join

once — this is why this ticket is blocked-by 038), extended with the intent lane:

every ledger outbound matches a released queue row or a declared intent. Unmatched

outbound → Attributed defect (Causing Job = send path), surfaced on the Fixer

Report / night watch like 038's mismatches. Derived checks, all from the same data:

unknown actor, Pending-Review→Sent gate bypass, per-actor release bursts,

hold-language-in-Reasoning released while its gate was unsatisfied.

Out of scope, related

The deeper cure for the hold-violation class — a real Hold Until/Release Gate

property on the queue so held rows never appear in the staff working set (today holds

are prose in Reasoning, invisible at click time) — is a production schema change

Junyan has not yet ruled on. Raise it at ruling time; do not build it here.

Resolution records: the send-engine hook line (Junyan pastes it — production Apps

Script), the intent-declaration convention + which skills write it, the reconciler

(where it runs, match rules), and the first unmatched-send-or-clean-week readout.

Build the completeness invariants (S, T) — unbilled bookings and no-shows

  • - T — a GTG booking with no arrival signal is surfaced

JOB 32 never derives a price (Junyan, 2026-07-23) binds S absolutely: the check asserts that a

money request exists, and never computes, suggests, or validates an amount. No fire-repair

authority (Ruling 2) — detect and escalate only.

Read guard for S (HOUSE-RULES): the Money Request Queue and ✅ Actions to Perform share one data

source (collection://20df225d-382f-4bb8-9c15-c31571c9f4e0). Any money read by collection id must

filter Type ∈ {Charge, Refund, Credit, Platform charge request, Invoice}, or Access/PIN, Decision,

Review and Run-Error rows come back as if they were charges.

U — a completed booking whose PBF never resolved. Added 2026-08-05 from

046's live read: seven bookings sat Complete + PBF = Unsent

for up to five days, unseen.

RULED by Junyan, 2026-08-05: a PBF more than 1 day past the booking is never sent. This is a

standing policy, not a per-case judgment — it makes U the sharpest check in the roster:

  • - The window is 1 day, hard. Not a tuning parameter, not something to pick after

050 reports. A booking Complete with PBF = Unsent

more than 24h after its booking date is a defect, full stop.

  • - The check is on resolution, not delivery. Sent and Will Not Send both clear it. U never

asserts that a message went out — only that somebody decided.

  • - Past the window there is exactly one correct resolution: Will Not Send. A late

"how did it go" reads worse than silence, and RESPONSE-RESOLVER step 2 forbids the tense mismatch

outright. So U's escalation text says mark it Will Not Send — it must never read as

"send the missing PBF", which would push staff into the one action the rule forbids.

Note what this rule does to the defect's shape: the damage is done at 24h and cannot be repaired

after. That makes U a deadline check, closer in kind to 042's ladder than to the rest of this

family — worth remembering if U ever starts to look like it wants escalation rungs.

Do NOT let U's escalation imply a send. The one way this check can do harm is by prompting a

stale PBF back out the door.

U is the only detector pointed at this event — confirmed by 050,

2026-08-05. Both existing tripwires are structurally incapable of firing on it: the Responder's

pass-ledger counters count skipped (step 7 ran every pass, so nothing ever climbed), and JOB 1

Step 5's "flag it if still Unsent the following day" has no query behind it — Step 1 pulls

today's rows only, and the 07-21 completeness sweep filters to Booking Status ∈ {Upcoming, In

Studio}, so a yesterday Complete + PBF = Unsent row is returned by neither. When U ships,

delete JOB 1 Step 5's prose flag or give it that query — an unfireable tripwire left in canon

beside a working check is worse than no rule, because it reads as coverage.

All three ship Provisional and graduate through 046's lane —

promotion gate, 5 consecutive clean review days.

#056

Merge the message-writing robot into the new loop

Details

Technical notes

Fold The Responder into the advancement loop

Question

The end-state is already ruled (018 §5, Junyan: *"because the

responder's scope is so narrow I'm okay folding it in — worst case scenario I would just draft the

responses"*). This ticket executes it, and owns the coordination the ruling deliberately deferred.

Why it folds. The Responder was carved out 2026-07-29 to get one measurable job with one

accountable owner: single scope, signed work, held-by-default, promotion ladder, scorecard. The

loop has all four, generalised — a courtesy reply is just a ticket whose loop is one turn long.

The Responder is the loop, hand-built early, for one action class. Keeping both means maintaining a

"who composes what" treaty forever, and every composer boundary is a place where steps go missing.

The evidence it produces defects. Dana Hufe (Tagvenue, 509A Mondays Sep 28–Nov 16): Junyan

hand-corrected the sequence because the composed message was missing the invoice line, the

pay-within-24h confirmation condition, and the terms link. A composer that only sees "reply to this

inbound" cannot know where a procedure is up to. Worse, because he sent it himself off-queue, the

correction taught the system nothing — precisely what the loop's rewrite beat exists to capture.

What this ticket must resolve:

  • - Coordination with the self-improving-desk sibling — the

Responder is that map's probe; its scorecard and nightly critic hang off it. Folding is a shared

decision, not a unilateral one. Read the sibling's state first; never double-extract.

  • - Latency parity — the Responder's ~30-min "a draft exists before a human reads the inbound" is

a real property. The loop must match it for this class before absorbing it.

  • - Class mapping — courtesy / acknowledgement, quick answers, holding replies on nudges,

PBF-adjacent replies, PBF initial sends, the no-show text (with its Ready to Send carve-out and

the absolute 1-day PBF expiry rule) become action classes in the queue, most of them

fast-promoted since they are already trusted.

  • - Do not retire before the successor acts (035): the Responder

keeps running until its classes demonstrably run in the loop.

Until this lands, the pilot boundary holds: process-driven messages (steps of an open ticket

— offers, confirmations, invoices, payment chases) belong to the loop, which holds the procedure

state; stateless replies stay with the Responder. "Stateless" means no process state, not

deterministic — the truly deterministic quick answers already left to the event-gate card lane

(033).

Technical notes

Rule promotion and demotion — the learning loop with a probation lane

Question

How the residue shrinks without rules rotting (decisions, decision 11). The debate's

major: promotion-only is the 063 self-sealing disease — a wrong committed rule is a

silent no-op forever. Build both directions:

evidence; nothing auto-commits.

  • - Approve in-session: Junyan reviews proposals in working sessions; approved

rules are committed to the rules file right there.

  • - Probation: new rules start Provisional (label+digest only — no archive, no

stub) with a residue-pass shadow-check before full power.

  • - Ceremony (064): every rule carries Rule | Committed (date+method) | Evidence.
  • - Demotion: daily sample of rules-archived mail guards against silent misfiles;

zero matches for M weeks → sunset flag surfaced for an in-session call.

One ladder philosophy shared with 018's promotion ladder — when 053's per-class

promotion numbers get picked (map fog), these should rhyme.

Technical notes

watchlist-refresh fails ~1 run in 4 and takes the J28 ledger pollers down with it

Question

From HANDOFF-concierge-ping-pong.md §6:

watchlist-refresh — the 30-min GitHub Action the J28 Gmail/SMS ledger pollers ride — fails

about one run in four. When it fails, SMS turns never reach the git ledger: during the David

Diep run his 21:54/21:57 exchange was absent, so an agent reading git-first canon saw no

Root cause is already known (handoff): scripts/sms_pull.py commits without checking whether an

earlier step left the tree mid-rebase with an unmerged watchlist/ file. Fix that concretely:

  1. 1. Guard the commit path — detect a dirty/mid-rebase tree, abort/repair (e.g. git rebase --abort

+ clean re-pull) instead of compounding it, and make each poller step fail independently rather

than poisoning the shared checkout.

  1. 2. Confirm the ~1-in-4 rate from the Actions run history, and re-measure after the fix (target:

a clean week).

  1. 3. Backfill the gaps: re-run the pollers over the failure windows so the ledger is complete

(idempotent-by-filename makes this safe by design — verify it holds).

  1. 4. A failed poller run must be visible: on failure, file the deduped Run Error row the 035

standing rule requires — today it fails silently, which is this map's third-time pathology.

Note the standing fog item "Scheduler reliability for the deterministic lane" — Actions cron

dropping runs is a separate, known problem with an external-tick pattern already prototyped

(send-engine/FbsTick.gs). This ticket fixes the crash, not the cadence; if the run history

shows dropped schedules are also material here, say so in the resolution and let the fog item

sharpen, don't absorb it.

Acceptance

  • - One clean week of watchlist-refresh runs (no rebase/unmerged-file failures).
  • - Ledger backfilled through the known gaps; David Diep's Aug-5 SMS turns present.
  • - A poller failure now files a Run Error row (verified by forcing one).

Resolution (2026-08-07)

The premise was wrong, and correcting it is most of the answer. watchlist-refresh does

not fail ~1 run in 4 at random, and the mid-rebase commit bug named in the handoff was not what

took the ledger down on Aug 5–6. Measured from the run history (gh run list, last 100 runs):

  • - One contiguous outage, 2026-08-06 02:00Z → 2026-08-07 02:00Z — essentially every 30-min

tick red or cancelled for ~24 hours.

  • - 38 consecutive green runs since 2026-08-07 12:00Z, through 00:30Z tonight.
  • - Scheduled ticks themselves were not dropped — every :00/:30 slot is present in the history

even through the outage. The fog item "Scheduler reliability for the deterministic lane" is

not implicated here; the crash was, and the hang below.

Three distinct causes in that window, none of them the mid-rebase bug (which was already

fixed on Aug 4 by 0f2e15a3, before the David Diep gap):

  1. 1. Canon-completeness check red — library/the-loop.md had no MIRRORS row, so

library_canon_check.py exited 1 and failed the whole run. Fixed by 136896ba; that fix is

what turned the lane green on Aug 7. A content gap, not a code bug.

  1. 2. A GitHub Actions infrastructure outage, ~15:00–17:30Z Aug 6 — runs died at

Failed to resolve action download info. Error: Service Unavailable, before any project step

ran, across five of Junyan's repos. Not ours to fix.

  1. 3. Ident-less rebase in the pollers — the real ledger-gap mechanism, fixed by this ticket.

sms_pull.py / gmail_pull.py passed a committer ident to git commit but not to the

git rebase in the push-retry loop. The runner has no global git identity, so a contested

push led to fatal: empty ident name (for <runner@...>), the rebase never resolved, the loop

re-pushed the same rejected head six times, and the run ended with the captured messages

uncommitted. Reproduced in a local sandbox (ident-less rebase: fatal: no email was given;

same rebase with -c user.name/-c user.email: rebases cleanly and lands our commit on top).

Also seen and worth naming: one run hung for 2h15m (31124777029), and the concurrency

group cancelled every tick behind it. That manufactured the "cancelled" pattern that had this

misread as a GitHub billing/quota ceiling for two passes.

Shipped — dc-canon (desk-correspondence) commit d1bea590:

  • - sms_pull.py, gmail_pull.py: IDENT passed to git rebase as well as git commit.
  • - gmail_pull.py: commit with check=False + warn, matching sms_pull, so a failed commit

can't kill the step or poison the shared checkout for the steps behind it.

  • - New scripts/run_error.py: files a deduped Type = Run Error row on ✅ Actions to Perform,

one open row per (workflow, step) — a repeat appends a dated line to that row instead of

fragmenting one root cause across rows. A row moved to Processed no longer matches, so a

fresh failure after a fix files fresh.

  • - watchlist.yml: id: on both poller steps + an if: failure() handler that names the failed

step and links the run.

Backfill — verified, nothing to re-run. threads/David-Diep-Skedda.md now carries the exact

turns the handoff said were missing: Wed Aug 5, 9:54 PM out, 9:57 PM in ×2. Idempotent-by-

filename holds, and the cursor rides in the same commit as the messages, so a lost push

re-captures on the next tick rather than skipping — which is why the gap self-healed.

Not verified in production: the Run Error row has never actually fired. Forcing one means

deliberately failing a live tick of the emergency-lane watchlist, which is not an unattended

action. Carried to ticket 066 with the clean-week measurement.

**Backfill audit, added same night (asked: did everything get backfilled?). SMS: all 200 Phone.com messages since Aug 4 23:45 matched to ledger pointers — 0 missing, both extensions. Gmail: 14 of 15 checked customer/platform turns present; one genuine hole** — Camille Quesada's 2026-08-06 19:32Z inbound, never polled (not outage-lost; the cursor rides in the message commit, so outage losses re-capture).
