✅#063🔎Checked every note that said 'the tool can't do this' — most were wrong2026-08-07
  • Fourteen of these notes were rounded up and tried for real, one by one
  • Three more turned out to be wrong, and one was simply out of date since last week
  • One was right: the booking system really has no way to look up a single booking on its own
  • The pattern is now clear — a note written by reading a tool's menu is usually wrong, and a note written after actually trying has always been right
  • Four notes couldn't be tested from here, so they now say so plainly instead of sounding certain
  • The notes are corrected, and there is a new house rule: say the date you tested it and how, or write UNTESTED
Technical notes

Audit canon's "the tool can't do X" claims — most were never tested

Question

Graduated from 059, whose own headline finding turned out to be

an instance of the pattern it was fixing.

059 established that Skedda HAS a write API and corrected four SOPs. It recorded one refusal:

"repricing an EXISTING booking — edit_booking has no price field." That was read off the

tool schema, not the wire. On 2026-08-06 a dry-run showed Skedda's booking PUT carries

price and paymentStatus, and client.update_booking merges {current, changes} with no

whitelist. The capability was always there; skedda-mcp just never exposed the parameter.

So the corrected claim is not "Skedda has no write API" but something more general and more

dangerous:

A missing tool argument is not evidence of a missing capability — but canon records it as one,
and then procedure is built on top of it.

Two more instances found the same day, both from a single line in CLAUDE.md

(*"Notion status-property view filters cannot be set through the API. Hand those to Junyan as a

manual edit"*):

  • - 💵 Processed-to-reconcile lost its Type filter when the view was recreated ~08-03. Canon

told agents to filter by hand in prose and handed Junyan a manual UI job. notion-update-view

set the three-part filter on the first attempt.

  • - 🤖 CC Open set (JOB 3/7) was quarantined — "robots must NOT use it" — for the same

stated reason. Also false, and the note was wrong in two further ways: the DB has no Status

property (it is Position) and no Resolved option (terminal states are Won/Lost/Done). It

predates the Position migration, so a human following it literally could not have fixed it

either. Now filtered; returned 22 correctly-open tickets on first read.

Three for three. Every claim tested so far has been false.

Why it matters

These are not documentation nits. Each one converted into work:

  • - staff time (a manual Notion edit that an API call does)
  • - degraded procedure (agents filtering in prose; a working view sitting unused)
  • - wrong automation tags — 059's original premise manufactured "a large share of the F1

mis-parked-gate family", gates raised for work no human needed to do

The failure mode is self-sealing: once written down, a capability claim is never retested, because

the claim itself is the reason nobody tries.

Scope

  1. 1. Enumerate the remaining untested "cannot / has no / not exposed" claims across CLAUDE.md,

CONTEXT.md, git canon library/, and the Notion Operating Library. Naive grep drowns in

legitimate operational rules — filter to claims about a tool, API, connector or endpoint.

  1. 2. Test each one against the wire, not the wrapper. A dry-run, a probe call, a schema fetch —

whatever would actually falsify it. Record the evidence either way; a confirmed limit is worth

as much as a refuted one, and is what stops the next re-test.

  1. 3. Fix what's false — the claim, every procedure built on it, and any automation tag it moved.
  2. 4. Rule on the general fix. Options, for Junyan: require every capability claim in canon to

carry the date and method of its test; or a periodic re-probe of recorded limits; or accept

drift and only test on contact. This is the part that stops recurrence, and it is a judgment

call about how much ceremony canon should carry.

Known candidates not yet tested: Alarm.com PIN masking (058 assumes it); gh absent on cloud

runners; notion-create-pages 404ing under the SOPs wiki root; Skedda's missing single-booking

GET; Gmail label: not matching user labels (this one HAS evidence — verified in the web UI

2026-08-06, and is the model for how the others should read).

Done when

Every tool-capability claim in canon is either tested with its evidence recorded, or explicitly

marked untested so the next agent knows not to trust it. Plus Junyan's ruling on scope 4.

Answer

CLOSED 2026-08-07. Full evidence: 063 capability-claim audit.

Enumerated across CLAUDE.md, CONTEXT.md, RUNTIMES.md, git canon library/*.md and the

Notion Operating Library CAPABILITIES page — which is where capability claims actually

concentrate, because recording them is its job. Fourteen tool/API/connector claims survived the

filter (operational "never" rules and read-only-by-design statements excluded — a policy is not

a capability claim).

Tested at the wire where the wire was reachable. Results:

  • - Confirmed: Skedda really has no single-booking GET — live GET /bookings/{id} returns

HTTP 405 (the path is PUT/DELETE only). The fetch-then-merge in update_booking is

necessary, not defensive. First claim in this family to survive a probe.

  • - Refuted / overstated (3): edit_booking "cannot reprice" was stale in two canon

locations (waive_booking_charge has existed since 08-06); skedda-mcp's "user.name/email

return null" is a list-endpoint artifact — the list carries firstName/lastName and the

single-user read returns the email as username; "images can't be re-inserted via API" confuses

a dead URL with a dead capability (notion-create-file-upload puts a downloaded copy back).

  • - Marked UNTESTED in place (4): Alarm.com PIN masking, Alarm.com's own write API, the

RemoteTrigger environment/env-var API, gh on cloud runners. None was probeable from this

harness (MFA-gated wire; in-process OAuth token; cloud-only runtime) — so each now says so

rather than reading as established fact.

  • - Not located (1): the notion-create-pages 404 under the SOPs wiki root appears nowhere in

canon. A claim nobody can find is a claim nobody can retest.

The sharper finding. Age doesn't predict which claims are wrong — provenance does. Every

claim read off a tool schema has been false or overstated (6 of 7 now, counting 059's three);

every claim tested when written (Codex↔Slack, Gmail label:, Phone.com international SMS) has

held. The rule isn't "retest old claims", it's "a schema is not a wire."

Fixed (commit 09f75da3, canon pushed): library/the-loop.md — reprice claim corrected with

the waive exception, Alarm.com write-API absence downgraded from fact to UNTESTED;

library/gotchas.md — image claim corrected, plus a new "Capability claims — test the wire, not

the wrapper" section carrying the rule and the standing untested list. Notion CAPABILITIES

page: skedda-mcp gaps re-tested and rewritten, alarm PIN masking flagged UNTESTED.

Scope 4 (the ceremony that stops recurrence) is NOT answered here — it is Junyan's ruling, and

it now has a sharper question than the one this ticket cut: not "how much ceremony should canon

carry" but "what do we require of a schema-derived claim specifically". Graduated as

064. The wire probes this harness couldn't reach graduated as

065; 058 gains an

opening step so it stops assuming the PIN limit it was built on.

✅#064⚖️Junyan ruled: every 'can't do this' note now lives in one table, with proof or the word UNTESTED2026-08-07
  • All the 'the computer can't do this' notes moved into a single table on the tools page
  • Each row says the date it was tried and how — or says UNTESTED in plain sight
  • An UNTESTED note doesn't count: if trying takes half a minute, the robot must try before handing Junyan the job
  • If trying is genuinely hard, the robot may still ask — but must admit the ask rests on an unchecked note
  • Later, the new step-by-step queue will refuse such asks automatically — a lock, not a reminder
Technical notes

What must a capability claim carry before it goes in canon — rule the ceremony

Question

Graduated from 063, and sharper than the question 063 cut.

063 expected "old claims rot, so schedule a re-probe". The evidence says otherwise: age doesn't

predict a wrong claim, provenance does. Every limit read off a tool schema has been false or

overstated (6 of 7 across 059 + 063); every limit tested when written has held. So a periodic

re-probe of everything is the wrong shape — it spends effort uniformly on a risk that is

concentrated in one place.

Junyan's ruling, on this narrower question: what does canon require of a claim that a tool

"cannot" do something?

Options, roughly in ascending ceremony:

  1. 1. Nothing new — the gotchas.md rule added by 063 (state date + method, or write UNTESTED) is

already in canon; accept drift and test on contact.

  1. 2. Provenance-tagged claims — every capability limit carries how it was established

(wire / schema / owner ruling), and a schema-derived one is never treated as binding: an

agent that hits it must probe before routing work to a human.

  1. 3. Schema-derived claims expire — they carry a date and are re-probed on a cadence (only this

class, not all claims); wire-tested ones stand until contradicted.

  1. 4. No untested limits in canon at all — a claim can't be written until it's probed; until then

it lives as an open question, not a rule.

Second half of the ruling: who pays for a probe. Options 2–4 all cost an agent a call it would

otherwise skip. Cheap for Skedda or Notion; not cheap for anything MFA-gated or cloud-only (see

065).

Why it matters

The failure mode is self-sealing — a written-down limit is the reason nobody retries — and it

converts directly into staff time, degraded procedure, and wrong automation tags (063's evidence).

This is the part that stops recurrence; everything else 063 did was cleanup.

Done when

Junyan has ruled, the rule is written into library/gotchas.md (extending the section 063 added),

and any automation tag that a refuted claim moved is re-checked.

Answer

CLOSED 2026-08-07 — Junyan ruled option C: register + probe rule now, lock when the queue lands.

  1. 1. Limits register, live on CAPABILITIES. Every "the tool cannot do X" claim is now a table

row — Limit | Tested (date + method) | Evidence — backfilled from 063's audit: 8 tested rows,

4 UNTESTED (both Alarm.com claims, the RemoteTrigger env API, gh on cloud runners). Refuted

rows are restated as what IS true, never deleted. New tools start every suspected limit at

UNTESTED. Loose prose limits are banned; gotchas.md keeps the principle and points here.

  1. 2. Probe rule B, in house-rules.md. An UNTESTED limit is not binding. Reachable from your

own harness in a few calls → probe first, fill the cell. Not reachable → gate as normal, but

the Action row must say "based on UNTESTED claim: <limit>".

  1. 3. The lock rides 053 (rider added): event-gate bounces

a proposal citing an UNTESTED limit. No separate chokepoint gets built — today gate-raises are

direct Notion writes with no code in between, so a lock before 053 would be a second queue

built to be thrown away.

  1. 4. Ruled out: the interim fuzzy prose-matching invariant sweep (noisy-invariant risk, the

July disease) and option D (no untested limits at all — would leave MFA-gated limits living

nowhere).

Canon commit 1fd1a3a9, pushed. Sign → column → lock, in that order, with the column live today.

✅#018🏗️The main robot's old job list was scrapped — replaced by a back-and-forth with Junyan2026-08-05
  • We went through its seven chores one by one, and almost none of them belonged to a robot that thinks
  • The alarm check is now a small program that runs every minute — waiting two hours to notice an alarm was silly
  • The who's-in-the-studio-today list and the staff-shift copying became small programs too
  • The mail sorter shrinks to one small job: open the message, start a ticket, pick the right procedure, stop
  • Everything after that becomes a loop: the robot suggests ONE next step, Junyan says yes or rewrites it, the robot does it, then checks it really happened
  • Rewriting a suggestion is how Junyan teaches it — the change he makes is the lesson, and the robot has to earn its way back to being trusted
  • Best part: if Junyan just does the job himself, the robot notices and ticks it off — no more telling the system what he already did
  • Charging money, sending someone out, and making door codes stay human forever, no matter how well the robot does
  • First trial run is on booking changes, with Junyan checking every step for the first week
Technical notes

Rearchitect J01 — ledger-fed triage, sub-job split, ticket-advancement contract

Extraction landed (2026-07-29, self-improving-desk ticket 003): the "draft responses" sub-job this rearchitecture deferred is now OUT of J01/JOB 2. The Responder (canon library/the-responder.md, cloud triggers trig_01WA9KLVfMD82gAYupxsBwH5 :20 + trig_015q8fhqzYvVjv5N6rnmH7wK :50, 30-min cadence 08:20–22:50 Toronto) exclusively owns non-ticket held replies: courtesy/acknowledgement replies, quick answers, nudge holding replies, PBF-adjacent replies. JOB 2 keeps inbox hygiene, classification, the audit log, and all ticket-forming/ticket-lifecycle replies — its courtesy-reply section is now a carve-out pointer. Design J01's sub-job split around that boundary.
Starvation mandate folded in (2026-08-05, 051): the tail
jobs JOB 3 / 4 / 31 (and 0 / 7) starve every pass — JOB 1/2 are mandatory, front-loaded, unbounded,
and eat the budget before the tail is reached (deferred-since counters only rise). Junyan ruled
split it up — the fix is this ticket's pass-split, not a new mechanism. Design constraint: the
tail must land where JOB 1/2 don't compete for its budget. Acceptance inherited: no job at
deferred-since >= 2 across 3 consecutive days; counters falling; JOB 1/2 mandatory halves still
complete on a heavy day.

Question

Design J01's next form with Junyan, from 017's evidence:

  • - Input: the J28 correspondence ledger replaces email triage as the intake (deterministic

ingest already classifies/lands the correspondence; J01 pulls from the ledger and advances

tickets).

  • - Split: does J01 decompose into sub-jobs, each owning one recurring action (advance alarm

codes / draft responses / prompt Ops lead for charges / …)? Coordinate with the

self-improving-desk sibling's micro-agent-split boundary — never double-extract; read the

sibling's state first.

  • - The advancement contract: the precise, checkable definition of "advance a ticket"

(which DB, which SOP link, which checkboxes) so wrong-place/wrong-SOP/wrong-checkbox defects

become detectable, not just annoying. Legacy SOPs flagged by 017 get fixed or retired as part

of the design.

  • - Cadence & home: which persona(s) the sub-jobs park in — driver: Junyan wants all jobs

parked in personas soon so he can focus on sales.

Design inputs captured from the Sweep Feedback backlog (2026-07-17)

The advancement-contract half of this ticket is exactly what a cluster of open 🔧 Sweep Feedback

design rows kept circling. Folded in here as design inputs so the contract is built to close them:

  • - Premature 'done' marking → orphan gates + premature Review advances (Sweep Feedback row

3997503281c481eea59af108599d8b30, now closed as superseded → this ticket). The canonical

"may-advance" rule the row proposes: a ticket may leave a gate or reach Position = Review

ONLY when a ground-truth read (all linked rows Processed/Sent) passes AND every checklist step

is checked/N-A; no prose-only gates (a named staff task without a linked Action row is a

defect the creating job must fix in the same write). This IS the checkable advancement contract.

  • - Two related rail bugs still OPEN in Sweep Feedback (not folded — smaller/mechanical, may be

quick tickets rather than waiting on the full rearchitecture): review_gate.py treats a

Scheduled queue row as an open gate (false-positive on FBS lifecycle sends), and charge/gate

rows born with blank Gate Type escape the machine-readable auto-clear. Both are "the gate

machinery trusts a mark instead of a ground-truth read" — the same thesis; the advancement

contract should subsume their fixes.

---

RESOLUTION — 2026-08-05: J01 dissolves

Grilled with Junyan across the full job list. The pass-split framing from

051 was answered by a bigger ruling: J01 does not get

re-sequenced, it gets dissolved. Walking the seven steps of the hourly pass one at a time,

Junyan ruled each out of the LLM pass — and what was left was not a job at all but a loop.

1. The deterministic lanes leave the pass

  • - JOB 1 alarm read — wrong venue entirely. "If there is an alarm it needs to be addressed

immediately by contacting staff, not by the Concierge 2 hours later." A 2-hourly alarm read is

theatre. The PENDING→ALARM pair check is a pure code diff and moves onto the event-gate tick

(minutes, pages staff directly), joining the live panel reads already there (invariants :25,

Doorman 04:00). The Concierge exits the alarm business.

  • - FAST-TRACK — deterministic. "Needs to be kept up to date throughout the day depending on

who's in the studio." Maintaining the who's-in-today + first-timer list derives from the FBS

board and the calendars — a renderer keeps it fresh all day (019 pattern). Judgment only enters

when someone on the list writes in, which is intake's job anyway.

  • - JOB 31 claimed-shift mirror — deterministic. Calendar mirror with dedup guards, zero

judgment. Plain code on a tick.

  • - JOB 1 remainder — redundant, dies. Junyan: "isn't the PBF auto queued already by the

responder — this whole section seems redundant." Correct: the Responder queues, JOB 3's

successor flips Sent, and invariant U (049) catches

unresolved ones. Nothing left to run.

2. JOB 2 splits — a thin triager, and everything else becomes the loop

Junyan: *"maybe it makes sense for an email triager to be separate and then its job is just to make

a ticket stub and do SOP routing (keep it very clean). Job 2 is too big."* So:

  • - The Triager — one small AI core: read inbound, make the ticket stub, SOP-route, log

correspondence, stop. Rails in front of it (who-is-this stamp, already-done check), rails behind.

This is the shape the Codex five-lanes artifact argued for and Junyan endorsed: move everything

possible to deterministic, keep one small AI core boxed in by checks that cannot forget.

  • - Everything downstream is not a job — it is a loop (below).

3. THE ADVANCEMENT CONTRACT — resolved, and it changed species

017's mandate was "make the advancement contract checkable, not more prose rules." The answer is

not a static may-advance checklist. Junyan: *"once a stub is created there needs to be some kind of

loop that keeps working until the ticket is fully resolved… a next action is described and then

accepted by staff or corrected… this then gets executed by the agent and the loop continues."*

The loop, five beats: PROPOSE → REVIEW (approve or rewrite) → EXECUTE (Ready rows only) →

VERIFY (ground-truth read) → NEXT.

  • - New Next Action queue DB, which RETIRES ✅ Actions to Perform. Junyan: "if we create a

purpose-built queue then we would retire actions-to-perform." One action surface, not two. One

open proposal per ticket: Proposed action (editable), Basis (the evidence read, so the

reviewer judges without re-investigating), Source SOP step, Status

Proposed → Ready → Done + Won't Do, Raised by, promotion counter per action class.

Reuses the Message Queue's proven verbs so staff learn nothing new.

  • - The rewrite IS the feedback. Approve-as-is or rewrite the action text, then mark Ready —

those are the only two moves. The diff between proposed and rewritten text is the correction

signal, and it feeds both the SOP fix and the promotion counter. This is what makes the contract

checkable at last: a wrong advance is caught before it happens, and the correction is captured

in-band instead of evaporating.

  • - Verify never trusts the write. Write, then read back from the live source; a mismatch is

raised, never marked Done. This subsumes the 017 rail bugs (review_gate.py's Scheduled-as-open

false positive, blank-Gate Type rows escaping the machine read) — both were "the machinery

trusts a mark instead of a ground-truth read", and the loop reads ground truth by construction.

  • - Promotion ladder, same as the Responder's. Every class starts human-gated; approved-unmodified

N times in a row earns auto-execute; any rewrite resets to zero. Money (charge/refund/credit/

pricing), physical dispatch, and alarm-code creation NEVER promote, whatever the counter says.

  • - Fast track from day one for classes already ruled safe and time-critical: instant booking

requests from Skedda and the platforms, and booking moves. Junyan: these "should be fast-tracked

immediately."

  • - Tick: a thin sweep every 5–10 min that reads the queue first and exits in seconds when

nothing is Ready and nothing waits — a full agent pass only when there is real work, which is

cheaper than the current fixed cadence that runs whether or not there is anything to do. A true

push path (event-gate fires the agent on approval) is a later prototype, not a prerequisite.

  • - Pilot on Booking Changes & Manual Help — highest traffic, where the F1/F2/F5 gate defects

concentrate, and the class whose actions the Skedda API makes genuinely executable. Junyan

reviews personally for week one (his corrections define the standard), Ops Lead after.

4. EXECUTOR-AGNOSTIC — the ruling that dissolves the back-capture tax

Junyan named the live pathology: *"rather than letting the agent do it, I want to execute everything

myself because I can do it faster and more reliably. This causes problems because now I need to

capture what I did back into the system."*

The loop answers this for free, because the verify beat reads ground truth and therefore does not

care who acted. Junyan moves the booking in Skedda himself → the next sweep's read sees it → the

row closes as Done (executed by human) and the loop advances. He sends the message himself → the

J28 pollers already caught it → same. A proposal overtaken by reality is closed, not nagged about.

His manual work is execution; the system's stance flips from "tell me what you did" to "I notice

what you did."

The residue is only what no read can see — a phone call's content, an in-person exchange, a

physical fix. Those keep the existing path: Junyan drops them into Slack #desk-drops, which

the Triager scans. He wants to need this less over time; it shrinks as the loop earns promotions,

and no new capture surface is built.

5. The Responder folds into the loop (end-state ruled, sequenced)

Surfaced by a live defect: Junyan hand-corrected a Responder-shaped sequence for Dana Hufe

(Tagvenue, 509A Mondays Sep 28–Nov 16) — the sent message needed the invoice line, the pay-within-

24h confirmation condition, and the terms link. Steps were missing because a composer that only

sees "reply to this inbound" cannot know where a procedure is up to. And because Junyan sent it

himself, off-queue, the correction taught the system nothing — the exact evaporation the loop's

rewrite beat exists to stop.

First principles: the Responder was carved out on 2026-07-29 to get one measurable job with one

accountable owner — single scope, signed work, held-by-default, promotion ladder, scorecard. The

loop has all four properties, generalised. A courtesy reply is just a ticket whose loop is one turn

long. So the Responder is not a different species; it is the loop, hand-built early, for one

action class. Maintaining a "who composes what" treaty between them is permanent overhead, and

every composer boundary is a place where steps go missing.

Junyan ruled the fold-in ("because the responder's scope is so narrow I'm okay folding it in —

worst case scenario I would just draft the responses"). Sequenced, not immediate, for three

reasons: the loop is unproven and the Responder works (never retire before the new owner

demonstrably acts — 035's standing lesson); the Responder is the self-improving-desk sibling's

probe, so folding it is a shared decision, not a unilateral one; and its ~30-min "draft exists

before a human reads the inbound" latency must be matched first. Until then the pilot boundary is

process-driven messages belong to the loop, stateless replies stay with the Responder — noting

"stateless" means no process state, not deterministic (the truly deterministic quick answers

already left to the event-gate card lane in 033).

6. The staff seam is an experiment, not a design

JOB 3 (reconcile) and JOB 4 (handback) are both, in Junyan's words, "related to figuring out what

staff has done" — passive inference hours after the fact. He wants "a much more interactive process

that can happen at a certain time in the day", and ruled it cannot be settled by grilling: *"this

needs to be tested out as this seam between agent and staff is very messy. We need to actually try

out different things to see what can work."* So it graduates as a series of prototype tickets,

not a designed answer.

7. Two premises corrected

  • - Skedda HAS a write API. 017's inventory and the Booking Changes SOPs all encode "Skedda has

no write API, so every create/move/cancel is [STAFF]" — a stale fact that manufactured much of

the F1 mis-parked-gate family. Junyan: "Skedda has a write API." Correcting it across canon is

its own ticket; the pilot establishes the truth for Booking Changes first.

  • - Alarm.com does not, and that is the risk to solve. Junyan's asymmetry: writing a code is low

risk, a renter unable to get in is high risk. A workaround is wanted, not accepted as a

permanent human gate.

8. Transition posture

A thin Concierge shell keeps the not-yet-rehomed jobs (34 overnight handoff, 0 feedback, 5

decay-flag review, 7 self-heal, 32 unpaid-charge scan, 33 end-of-day review) until each finds its

lane, and is killed when empty. Nothing retires before its successor demonstrably acts.

051's acceptance criteria transfer to the pilot: no job at deferred-since >= 2 across 3

consecutive days, counters falling, and intake still complete on a heavy day.

Design artifacts: the ticket advancement loop, walked end to end

(interactive — the Nadia Okonkwo booking-move example, with the rewrite beat and the promotion

counter live) · why the tail starved.

Graduated: 053 loop build (the head of the frontier) ·

054 triager · 055 deterministic rehomes ·

056 Responder fold-in ·

057 staff-seam experiments ·

058 Alarm.com write workaround ·

059 Skedda write-API canon correction.

✅#044🧾The last retired-robot checks got a home — and a review desk2026-08-05
  • Two robots were retired. Some of their checks never got re-homed. These are those
  • Ten new checks designed: does yesterday's paperwork exist, do the lists agree, did anyone go unbilled
  • Every one has a named owner — a check with no owner is how the lockout happened
  • Big rule: a new check starts on probation. It reports quietly, and a person marks each finding real or noise
  • Five clean review days in a row and it earns the right to raise a flag on its own
  • Except the list-matching checks — those stay on probation forever, because two lists disagreeing is always a judgement call
  • Build order: the four paperwork checks go first, alone. The rest follow once those are proven

Rehome the bookends' remaining checks — existence, reconciliation, completeness

Question

The residue of 035. That ticket settled the escalation half —

the deadline-aware access ladder, now 042. It deliberately did

not settle the rest of the Opener's and Closer's verification layer. This ticket owns that.

Unblocked 2026-08-04: 043 closed — the checker

runs in the cloud inside event-gate (POST /invariants/<token>, Cloud Scheduler desk-invariants

at :25), survives a closed laptop, escalates a defect seen 3 runs in a row to an ✅ Actions row, and

files a Run Error row if it cannot run. New invariants designed here land as checks in

dc-canon/services/event-gate/invariants.py and inherit all of that for free.

Design with Junyan, check by check, from the Opener's old Steps 3/4/6/6B/6C/7/7B/8:

  • - Existence invariants (IPR / Overtime / FBS itinerary / watchlist freshness / Day Sheet

freshness): pure date-filtered queries. The Sentinel covers run heartbeats; these cover

artifact existence — decide whether that is one roster or two.

  • - Reconciliation invariants (FBS board ↔ message queue ↔ calendars; duplicate/orphan board

rows; yesterday's hours vs Staff Scheduling): deterministic diffs, with the Custodian's 09:35

pass as the human-facing reviewer of whatever gets flagged.

  • - Completeness sweeps ([Unpaid] → Money Request Queue including the prior-day back-check;

GTG no-show surfacing): mostly date math and plan rates — a small script on the deterministic

lane, with only the discretionary residue routed to Actions rows. JOB 32 never derives a

price (Junyan, 2026-07-23) still binds anything touching money.

  • - Escalation contract: every failed check lands where a human already looks — Actions

For = Ops Lead / For = Junyan per the 027 split, or the Fixer Report. "Check exists" is not

done until "failure is seen" is wired. 035 proved this is the part that gets skipped.

  • - No fire-repair authority (Ruling 2 stands): invariants detect and escalate; the Ops lead

investigates. Nothing auto-refires generators.

Carry 035's standing lesson in: a retirement is not done until every step of the retired job

names its new owner. Each check designed here closes with a named owner, or it does not ship.

Resolution — 2026-08-05

All three families ship, and they ship behind a human review lane. Junyan ruled against the

existence-first split once the loop was on the table: the reason to stagger the families was that

you cannot soak ten new checks at once and still know which one is noisy — a probation lane answers

that directly, because every check's findings are marked real-or-noise by a person before the check

is ever allowed to page anyone. The review lane replaces the soak, so the split loses its purpose.

The probation lane (new machinery — the governing decision here)

Every invariant carries a maturity: Provisional or Escalating.

  • - A Provisional check runs on the normal :25 pass and writes its findings to a Provisional block

on the 🩺 Desk Invariants page. It files no ✅ Actions row, pages nobody, and does not feed

the 3-runs-in-a-row escalation counter.

  • - The Custodian's 09:35 pass presents each provisional finding to a human, who marks it real

or noise.

  • - A check graduates to Escalating after 5 consecutive review days with zero noise marks,

and inherits 043's full escalation machinery unchanged. Any noise mark resets the counter to zero

and the check goes back for a fix.

  • - 045's two standing rules bind every check regardless of maturity: a day of live data before it may

escalate (now subsumed — graduation is strictly stronger), and no check ever counts its own

output as desk state (the Raised by == SELF exclusion that C and B needed).

Graduation is not universal. Junyan's ruling splits it by family:

  • - Existence + Completeness — promotion gate. Binary checks that graduate and then run

autonomously. A missing report is missing; there is nothing left for a human to judge once the

check is proven correct.

  • - Reconciliation — permanent seat. These never graduate. A board↔calendar diff is a judgment

call forever: "these two disagree" is usually a timing artifact, and no amount of clean history

makes the next disagreement self-evident. Reconciliation findings land in front of the Custodian's

09:35 reviewer every day, permanently, and only a human-marked-real finding becomes an Actions row.

This is the check class that would otherwise get its battery ripped out.

The check roster — each with its named owner

Carrying 035's standing lesson: a check that does not name an owner does not ship.

Existence (from Opener Steps 3/4/6B) — pure date-filtered queries, escalate to For = Ops Lead:

| Code | Check | Owner on failure |

|---|---|---|

| K | Yesterday's IPR exists | Ops Lead — regenerate |

| L | Yesterday's Overtime report exists | Ops Lead — regenerate |

| M | Today's FBS itinerary exists | Ops Lead — regenerate |

| N | Day Sheet build stamp is fresher than 45 min | Ops Lead — investigate the renderer |

One roster or two — ruled two, kept separate. The Sentinel watches runs; these watch

artifacts. They are not the same question and must not be merged: a run that reports success while

producing nothing is precisely the failure mode that hid the dead checker for five days, and only an

artifact check catches it. Watchlist freshness is not in this roster — it is already J29-as-code

(closed in 011); do not double-implement it.

Reconciliation (Steps 6/6C/7B) — deterministic diffs, permanent human seat at 09:35:

| Code | Check | Owner on human-marked-real |

|---|---|---|

| P | Every good-to-go FBS booking has its due HTA/EOB/AVA queued | Ops Lead |

| Q | FBS board ↔ calendars: no duplicate rows, no orphan rows | Ops Lead |

| R | Yesterday's logged hours ↔ Staff Scheduling | Ops Lead (Timekeeper's line) |

Completeness (Steps 7/8) — date math and plan rates, promotion gate:

| Code | Check | Owner on failure |

|---|---|---|

| S | Every [Unpaid] booking has a Money Request Queue row, including the prior-day back-check | Ops Lead |

| T | A GTG booking with no arrival signal is surfaced | Ops Lead |

JOB 32 never derives a price (Junyan, 2026-07-23) binds S absolutely: the check asserts that a

money request exists, and never computes, suggests, or validates an amount. No fire-repair

authority (Ruling 2) binds all ten: they detect and escalate, nothing auto-refires a generator.

Still open, carried forward

The PBF backlog question is not answered here — confirming whether JOB 33 has actually been

clearing it since Jul 22, or whether the one-off catch-up sweep is still outstanding, needs a live

Notion read that this session did not run. Carried explicitly onto 046.

Graduated: 046 — the probation lane (blocks both builds),

047 — existence + completeness checks,

048 — reconciliation checks.

✅#050🔍We found out why thank-you messages stopped going out2026-08-05
  • The robot was doing its job. It found every finished booking and wrote the thank-you the same hour
  • But a thank-you is not sent by the robot. It waits for a person to press send
  • Seven of them waited, and nobody pressed send. After one day they can never be sent
  • The ones a person did release went out in 15 to 45 minutes, so nothing is broken further down
  • The robot shows the waiting list once a night, at 10:50pm. Miss that night and it is never shown again
  • One booking was a real miss: Sarra's Jul 31 event ended at 11:30pm, after the last check of the day
  • The two alarms meant to catch this could never have gone off — they were watching the wrong thing
  • The board is clean now. Next: Junyan decides who presses send, or if the robot should just send it
Technical notes

The Responder's PBF sweep is missing completed bookings — diagnose it

Question

Surfaced 2026-08-05 by the live read on 046. Seven bookings

sit Booking Status = Complete with PBF = Unsent, and every one of them dates from 2026-07-31 or

later — the exact day PBF initial sends moved from Concierge JOB 33 to The Responder. The

pre-handoff backlog is clean (Jun 28 → Jul 30 all resolved), so this is not an old pile: it is a new

leak that started at the handoff and has run continuously for five days.

Why does this need diagnosis rather than a fix? Because the desk already had a tripwire for it and

the tripwire did not fire. Two of them, in fact:

  • - library/job-1-adt-alarm.md Step 5: a Complete + PBF = Unsent booking still Unsent *the

following day* means the Responder's sweep is broken — flag it.

  • - library/the-responder.md pass step 7 + the 🧾 PASS LEDGER rule: the sweep must report

PBF: 0 found · no-show: 0 found every pass, precisely so a silent no-op is distinguishable from

a sweep that never ran, with 3-consecutive → run-first and 5-consecutive → Attributed defect.

Four of these rows are five days past the JOB 1 line. So either the sweep is not running, or it is

running and reporting 0 found while seven qualifying rows sit in plain sight, or it is filing the

escalation and nobody reads it. Establish which before changing any code — the three have very

different fixes, and 045's lesson is that guessing at a mechanism produces a confident wrong answer.

Read the Responder's rolling reports for 2026-07-31 → 2026-08-05 and answer, in order:

  1. 1. Did the Responder run its passes at all over that window? (Run Monitor + Workflow Reports.)
  2. 2. Does step 7 appear in the pass ledger each pass, and what does it report — ran, skipped, or a

count?

  1. 3. If it reports a count, why do these seven rows not match its query? Candidate causes worth

checking specifically: the AI Monitor — Today view's absolute-date filter (HOUSE-RULES warns

the relative "today" filter silently returns the wrong day past ~20:00 Toronto — and the sweep

only looks at today's rows, so a booking that flips to Complete after the last pass of its

own day may never be seen again); the cross-status dedup skipping rows it shouldn't; or

Type of Booking filtering.

  1. 4. Did the 3-consecutive / 5-consecutive escalation ever fire? If it did, where did the row go and

why did no one act on it — that is a second, separate defect from the sweep itself.

Suspicion worth testing first, not assuming: the sweep reads the Today view, but a PBF becomes

due when a booking completes, which is often the last pass of the day or after it. A same-day-only

window with a 30-min cadence has an obvious hole at the day boundary — and the leak starting exactly

at the handoff is consistent with the old owner (JOB 1, hourly, with an every-sweep backstop

explicitly written to catch bookings that completed in an earlier sweep) having covered that hole

in a way the new owner does not.

Not this ticket's job: the eight outstanding rows themselves. Junyan ruled 2026-08-05 that a

PBF more than 1 day past the booking is never sent — so all eight are Will Not Send, with no

judgment left in any of them. Do not send a single one as part of diagnosing this.

That ruling raises the stakes on the diagnosis. If a PBF is unsendable after 24h, then every

pass the sweep misses is a customer message permanently lost, not merely delayed — there is no

catch-up sweep that can repair it later. The 30-min cadence bought nothing on those seven bookings.

Whatever the mechanism turns out to be, the fix has to close the window inside a day, and a

"backfill the backlog" remedy is worthless here by construction.

Feeds 049's check U, whose window the ruling now fixes at a

hard 24h — so U no longer waits on this ticket to be specified, only to be built alongside it.

Resolution — 2026-08-05

**The sweep is not broken. The premise is wrong twice over, and the real defect is one step

downstream: a PBF is queued by a robot but released by a human, and nobody was releasing them.**

Evidence: the Responder's rolling dailies for 2026-07-31 → 2026-08-05 (six pages, every 30-min

pass), the 📤 Message Queue's PBF rows since 2026-07-29 (live SQL), and the AI Monitor board rows

for 2026-07-29 → 2026-08-08 (live SQL).

1. Did the Responder run its passes? — Yes, all six days, every pass

A rolling daily exists for each of 07-31, 08-01, 08-02, 08-03, 08-04, 08-05, each with a continuous

30-minute pass sequence (08:20 → 22:50). No missing-report day, no silent gap. The 09:50 pass on

08-05 escalated that the Concierge had not run — that is a different robot and a separate

incident (it started at 14:18Z).

2. Does step 7 appear each pass, and what does it report? — Present every pass, with real counts

Not skipped, not a manufactured 0 found. The counts move with the board, and from 08-05 the

pass ledger emits the machine line (LEDGER-V1 job=STEP 7 state=ran since=0 …) as well. Sample:

  • - 07-31 16:50 — PBF: 2 found · 2 queued · no-show: 1 found · 1 queued
  • - 07-31 18:51 — PBF: 3 found · 1 queued (two deduped)
  • - 08-01 14:21 — PBF: 1 found · 1 queued, explicitly noting every earlier pass reported 0 found

because nothing had reached Complete yet

  • - 08-05 all passes — PBF: 0 found · no-show: 0 found · PBF expired: 0, with the absolute date

filter verified on the returned rows every time

The AI Monitor Today-view date filter — the ticket's named suspect — is being handled correctly.

Every pass either re-asserts the absolute date or verifies the returned rows carry it; the 08-05

08:50 pass caught it stale at 2026-08-04 on arrival and reset it before reading. No pass read the

wrong day.

3. Why did the seven rows not match its query? — They did match. It queued them; they were never released

The queue tells the story. Every one of the leaked bookings has a PBF row created by

Raised by = The Responder, same day, usually within the hour of the booking completing:

| Booking | PBF row created | Sent At |

|---|---|---|

| Vladimir Sidorkin (509A, Jul 31) | 07-31 20:56Z | never |

| Samara I. (509B, Jul 31) | 07-31 22:57Z | never |

| Amandeep Kaur (693, Jul 31) | 08-01 00:26Z | never |

| Jake Maresca (509A, Aug 1) | 08-01 18:26Z | never |

| Julia S. (901, Aug 1) | 08-01 20:54Z | never |

| Kristel San Jose (901, Aug 3) | 08-04 02:25Z | never |

| Eunyi C (Aug 4) | 08-04 15:27Z | never |

Against the ones that worked in the same window — Melisa Pustilnik (sent 43 min after queueing),

Sierra Goulding (26 min), Dee Adelubi Brown / Ayden Mauro / Tanisha Chakma (15 min). The send

engine is healthy (already established by 045: median lag from

Send After to Sent At = 0.0 min). The difference between a PBF that arrives and a PBF that dies

is entirely whether a human released it from Pending Review.

So the mechanism is hold-and-forget, and it is self-concealing:

  • - canon holds every PBF at Pending Review — never auto-send (the-responder.md Scope; AUTONOMY);
  • - the cross-status dedup correctly skips any booking that already has a row in any status, so

from the next pass onward the sweep reports found · deduped — forever, truthfully, and

indistinguishably from a healthy pass;

  • - the courtesy/PBF approval block is emitted on the 22:50 pass only, so a held PBF gets exactly

one appearance in front of a human on the night it is drafted, and none after.

The ticket's leading suspicion — the same-day window with a hole at the day boundary — is not

the mechanism for these seven; every one was found and queued on its own day. It is real but rarer:

Sarra Genevieve (509A, Jul 31, FBS event 18:45–23:30) has no queue row at all — she completed

after the day's last pass, and no later pass ever looks at a past date. She is the single true

sweep miss in the window, and she is the case Junyan ruled Will Not Send on 08-05.

Two other rows in the map's eight were never leaks: Divine Mokuye is a Studio Viewing (field

never flipped), and the Jun 27 straggler predates the handoff.

4. Did the 3/5-consecutive escalation ever fire? — No, and it could not have

Both tripwires are structurally incapable of firing on this failure:

  • - The Responder's pass-ledger counters count skipped, not found-and-unresolved. Step 7 ran

every pass, so deferred-since stayed 0 and no counter ever climbed. The ledger was built to

distinguish a silent no-op from a sweep that never ran — a real gap, correctly closed — but the

failure that actually happened is a third state the contract has no name for: ran, found it,

queued it, and it died in review.

  • - JOB 1 Step 5's next-day flag has no query behind it. Step 1 pulls today's rows only, and the

07-21 completeness sweep queries only Booking Date < today AND Booking Status ∈ {Upcoming, In

Studio}. A Complete + PBF = Unsent booking from yesterday is returned by neither. The rule

says "flag it if still Unsent the following day" and nothing the job reads can ever see it.

So the finding is not "nobody read the tripwire" — it is that neither tripwire was watching this

event. That is a sharper and more uncomfortable answer than the ticket's framing: the desk had

two detectors pointed at a sweep that was working, and none pointed at the review queue where the

messages were actually dying.

State as of this session

Live SQL over the AI Monitor board returns zero Complete + PBF = Unsent rows for

2026-07-29 → 2026-08-05. All eight are resolved under Junyan's 1-day rule. Nothing outstanding.

What falls out

  • - The fix is a release policy, not sweep code — graduated as

052. Under the 1-day rule a held PBF has ≤24h to be released or

it is dead, and the current path gives it one 22:50 appearance. Junyan's call: auto-send PBFs

(as the no-show text already does), or escalate an unreleased one before its window closes.

  • - 049's check U is confirmed as the right detector and its

design is unchanged by this — it asserts resolution, which is exactly the event both existing

tripwires miss. Noted there that JOB 1 Step 5's prose rule needs deleting or backing with a query

once U ships; two unfireable tripwires should not survive the arrival of a working one.

  • - The pass-ledger contract has a blind spot worth generalising beyond PBF: a step that finds

work, hands it off correctly, and never sees it resolved reports a clean pass forever. Banked in

the map's fog, not ticketed — the registry's produces / verify_within fields

(001) may already cover it.

Question

Source handoff: HANDOFF-2026-08-04-concierge-tail-starvation.md.

Across recent Concierge passes the same tail jobs are deferred every pass and never run. As of

the 2026-08-04 20:10 HEAVY-PM pass: JOB 3, JOB 4, JOB 31 at deferred-since = 2; JOB 0, JOB 7

at 1. Structural, not incidental: JOB 1 (alarm reconciliation) and JOB 2 (inbox triage) are

mandatory, front-loaded, and unbounded — they scale with the day's activity and consume the

pass budget before the tail is reached. "Defer with a named first slice" records the debt but

never pays it; nothing in the loop causes an aged job to win.

Same failure class as 037 (report intake sacrificed under context

pressure); that ticket fixed which step got sacrificed by reordering — this one is about the

steps that are permanently last.

Junyan to pick one:

  1. 1. Cap the front half — timebox/size-cap JOB 1 / JOB 2 so the tail always gets a slice.

Fixes the cause; risks leaving alarm/inbox work half-done.

  1. 2. Reassign by pass type — dedicated pass slot for JOB 3 / 4 / 31; costs a new trigger +

RUNTIMES.md entry. Most robust if tail work keeps growing.

  1. 3. Escalate on age — any job at deferred-since >= 2 is promoted above the optional half

of JOB 1 / JOB 2 next pass. Smallest self-correcting change; requires the routine to mark its

mandatory vs optional sub-steps explicitly (it currently does not).

Acceptance

  • - No job sits at deferred-since >= 2 across three consecutive days.
  • - Rolling daily report: every JOB line attempted or deferred with the counter falling.
  • - JOB 1 and JOB 2 still complete their mandatory halves on a heavy day.

✅#043🔌The safety checker is alive again, and in the cloud2026-08-04
  • The hourly checker had been dead since Jul 29 — 84 crashes in a row, and nothing said so
  • The cause: a Notion field it asks about every run was deleted, so every run failed
  • Worse, its report page kept showing the last good day, so it still looked healthy

Technical notes

The invariants checker is dead and Mac-bound — repair it and move it to the cloud

Question

Surfaced while grilling 035 on 2026-08-03. Urgent — this is a

live blind spot, not a planned improvement.

What is broken

  • - desk_invariants.py (v1, hourly launchd ca.danceannex.desk-invariants.plist on Junyan's Mac)

is crashing every run. desk_invariants.log holds 122 tracebacks, most recent

2026-08-01 10:25.

  • - Every one is the same: HTTP 400 from Notion at desk_invariants.py:101 —

query(DS_CC, {"property": "Status", "status": {"does_not_equal": "Resolved"}}) against the

Customer Care data source. Almost certainly a renamed property, or Status retyped from

status to select. Confirm against the live schema before patching.

  • - desk_invariants_v2.py was never swapped in. The plist still points at v1; v2 has sat

untouched since Jul 22, its Phase 0 go-ahead never given.

So the desk has had no invariants checker running at all, for an unknown stretch, and nothing

reported that. This is the Vicky lockout's exact pathology one level up: the thing that watches

for silent failure was itself failing silently.

What this ticket must do

  1. 1. Establish the blackout window. When did v1 last complete a clean run? What did the 🩺 Desk

Invariants page show during the gap, and did anything read it and believe it? A stale page that

still renders "healthy" is worse than a blank one.

  1. 2. Fix the query. Diff the checker's assumed Customer Care schema against the live one.
  2. 3. Move it to the cloud. Junyan's ruling in 035: "everything needs to be in the cloud."

A Mac-bound launchd checker is not an acceptable home — laptop lid, sleep

(local-tasks-miss-runs-on-sleep), and no visibility while he is away. Decide the venue:

folding into event-gate (as 042 does) versus its own

Cloud Run job. GitHub Actions is disqualified for anything deadline-shaped (cron drops runs).

  1. 4. Settle the v1 → v2 question in the same move. v2 adds structured defects with stable keys,

persistence state, 3-consecutive-run auto-escalation to an Actions row

(Raised by = Desk Invariants — the picklist option added by 035's rehoming pass), trend

history, and invariant I (verified-send vs the J28 ledger). Migrating a broken v1 to the cloud

unchanged would be wasted work. Junyan's explicit go is still required for the swap.

  1. 5. The checker must report its own health. It has to be impossible for this to happen again

unnoticed: a run that cannot complete files a Type = Run Error row, and a checker that has not

reported inside its cadence is itself a Sentinel-visible missing run.

Why it is urgent

044 parks the bookends' existence, reconciliation, and

completeness checks here — that ticket cannot start until this checker is alive and hosted

somewhere trustworthy. And every day it stays dead, the desk is running with its smoke detector

unplugged.

---

Resolution — 2026-08-04

The checker is alive, hosted in the cloud, and now reports its own health.

1. The blackout window

Last clean run 2026-07-29 13:25 -03 (the 🩺 page's own heading — it had been serving that

five-day-old snapshot ever since, complete with 10 defects, reading exactly like a healthy run;

nothing on the page said how old it was). 84 crashed runs followed: 72 × HTTP 400 from the

CC query, and 12 × DNS failure — the laptop asleep or offline, the Mac-bound half of the

problem showing up in the evidence rather than the argument. Nothing else read the page during

the gap that we can attribute, but nothing could have told the difference either — which is

the finding that matters.

2. The query

Not a rename and not a retype: the Customer Care DB's Status property has been deleted

outright. CC now carries Position alone, with the same option set as Sales

(Routing / In Progress / Gate:* / Review / Won / Lost / Done). The query is now the Sales

shape — open = Position not in Won/Lost/Done, Review deliberately still open, because

finishers stop there. Invariant F (Status=Resolved vs Position) is retired: with no

Status property, Position cannot disagree with it.

3. The venue — folded into event-gate (Junyan's ruling)

Same Cloud Run service as 042's ladder: one container, one

NOTION_TOKEN, one deploy, one health route to watch, rather than a second thing that can die

quietly. POST /invariants/<GATE_PATH_TOKEN> (?dry_run=true to read without writing), fired by

its own Cloud Scheduler job *desk-invariants, 25 8-22 America/Toronto. The route is

read-and-report, so the SMS lane's GATE_DISABLED does not gate it — INVARIANTS_DISABLED=1 is

its own kill switch. GitHub Actions stayed disqualified.

4. v1 → v2 — v2 shipped (Junyan's go)

invariants.py in dc-canon/services/event-gate is the v2 port: stable defect keys, run

persistence, 3-consecutive-run escalation to an ✅ Actions row (Raised by = Desk Invariants,

For = Ops Lead), trend history, and invariant I. Three things changed in the move, each because

the cloud is not a Mac:

  • - State lives on the 🩺 page**, as chunked JSON code blocks — Cloud Run instances are

ephemeral, and a bucket would have meant another credential. *(Caught in flight: at 27 defects

the state overran Notion's 2000-char text cap and parsed back as a cold start — run counts

reset every pass, escalation silently never fires. Exactly this ticket's disease. Now chunked,

with history dropped before defects if it ever overflows, and tested.)*

  • - Escalation is capped at 5 rows per run, most-persistent first. A five-day backlog would

otherwise have landed as 27 rows in one pass, which is how a list gets ignored.

  • - Invariant I is skipped, not alarmed, where the J28 ledger is unreachable (INV_LEDGER_URL

unset today); the 🩺 page says so on every run. An unreachable ledger is a venue fact, not a

desk defect. Wiring the ledger URL is the one piece left — see the fog note on the map.

5. Self-health

A run that cannot complete files one Type = Run Error row (Raised by = Desk Invariants,

For = Ops Lead), deduped against the open row — 84 broken runs file one row, not 84, but never

zero. The next clean run marks that row Processed, so a stale alarm can't linger either.

Verified end-to-end against live Notion (filed, deduped, cleaned up). 168 tests green.

Cutover

launchd ca.danceannex.desk-invariants unloaded, plist renamed .RETIRED-2026-08-04, and

both Mac scripts carry 🚦 RETIRED / DO-NOT-RE-ENABLE banners (kept as manual fallback only).

Shipped in desk-correspondence commit a5e11ae; Cloud Run revision event-gate-00017-nls.

What it found on day one

27 defects on the first live cloud runs, 5 already escalated to the Ops Lead — including the

Studio 693 alarm-trigger ticket sitting 48h+ untouched and, most urgent, 16 message-queue

rows stuck at Ready to Send (dispatch is seconds; one has been waiting ~10 days). The send

engine is not draining the queue. That is a live desk problem this checker's absence was hiding —

flagged to Junyan, not fixed here.

✅#045🐺The checker cried wolf — now it doesn't2026-08-04
  • Yesterday's alarm said 16 approved messages were stuck and never sent
  • They were not. Every one was a message scheduled for later — that's a feature
  • The 10-day-old one was simply waiting for its August 6 date, exactly as asked
  • Measured properly, the sender hits its times to the minute. It was never broken

Technical notes

The checker cried wolf — invariant G counted scheduled sends as stuck

Question

Cut 2026-08-04 as *"the send engine is not draining the queue — 16 messages stuck at Ready to

Send"*, on the repaired checker's first live report (043).

That premise was wrong, and the ticket's real subject is the checker. Investigating it first

is what this ticket did; the finding inverted it.

Resolution — 2026-08-04

The send engine is healthy. Invariant G was lying. Three self-inflicted false alarms found

and fixed; the first day's report went from 27 defects to 11, all genuine.

1. G ignored Send After — the queue's scheduled-send feature

The 📤 queue supports scheduled sends: EOB, AVA, and dated payment check-ins sit at

Ready to Send with a future Send After by design. G measured every such row as stuck.

The headline "~10 days stuck" row — Amandeep Singh Bharaj — Aug 6 payment check-in, created

Jul 25 — was correctly waiting for its Aug 6 09:00 date. Nothing needed rescuing; no message

was late; no row needed a human decision about sending it late, which was to be step 1 of

this ticket.

Measured over the 22 scheduled rows dispatched since Aug 3: median lag from Send After to

Sent At = 0.0 minutes. The engine (029) hits its times

exactly. The overnight 2 → 16 climb was the evening's EOB/AVA rows queueing for their slots, and

by morning they had all gone out on schedule.

2. G measured from creation, not release

A row is created at Pending Review and released to Ready to Send later, so age-since-creation

charged human review time (median 64 min) to the engine. Notion records no release timestamp, so

G now measures from Send After when set, else the row's last edit — which is the Status flip

in the ordinary case, and errs safe: a genuinely undispatched row stops being edited, so its age

keeps growing. Threshold raised 10 → 15 minutes, to absorb clock skew and one missed poll.

3. The checker was generating its own defects

Exposed once G went quiet: invariant C flagged the checker's own escalation rows (blank

Gate Type, linked to a gate ticket) — which escalate into more rows, which trip C again. And

the mirror image: invariant B was being silenced by those same rows — "this gate ticket has

no open Action row" was cleared by the row the checker had filed about that very absence, so an

orphaned gate would go quiet forever without a human ever raising the real gate row. Rows with

Raised by = Desk Invariants are now excluded from both.

Shipped: 174 tests green, commit ba978e6e, revision event-gate-00018-hh7.

The lesson worth keeping

A checker that cries wolf is worse than no checker — it teaches the desk to skim past its page,

which is a slower version of the blackout 043 just fixed. 43 of the first 44 alarms were noise

(16 G + 9 C + 2 B suppressions). Any new invariant designed in

044 must be tested against a day of live data before it is

allowed to escalate to a human, and must never count its own output as desk state.

Still standing from the first report: the Studio 693 alarm-trigger ticket (open, untouched

48h+, sitting at Gate: Staff with no action row) and the Aug 1 Studio 509 door-left-open

ticket. Those were real, and they are on the Ops Lead's list.

  • So the problem isn't spotting things — it's that a warning can sit and go stale
  • DONE: jobs the two retired robots used to do now have new owners
  • (billing scan, thank-you texts, staff hours, staff shift emails — all re-homed)

Technical notes

Rebuild the bookends' verification layer as invariants — the Opener's checks get a durable home

RESOLUTION 2026-08-03 — the escalation half is designed; build graduates to 042

Grilled with Junyan. Defect (B) — *"a correctly-raised urgent flag sat in a queue 13.5h with

nothing re-checking it"* — now has a full design, ruled end to end. Every open question in the

"Still open" block below is answered.

Ground finding that reframed the ticket: the interim coverage this ticket leaned on is dead.

desk_invariants.py (v1, hourly launchd on the Mac) has 122 tracebacks, latest 2026-08-01 10:25,

all HTTP 400 on a Notion Status does_not_equal Resolved query against the Customer Care data

source — almost certainly a renamed/retyped property. The plist still points at v1;

desk_invariants_v2.py was never swapped in (untouched since Jul 22). So no invariants checker has

been running at all, and nobody noticed — the same failure as the Vicky lockout, one level up.

Graduated as 043.

Rulings

  1. 1. Cloud, not the Mac (Junyan: *"I want to move it to the cloud — everything needs to be in the

cloud"*). The Jul 25 ruling ("the re-check lives in desk_invariants_v2") stands on substance

— deterministic checker, not an LLM pass — but its venue is overruled: a Mac-bound launchd

script is not an acceptable home for a renter-facing deadline check, and v1 just proved why.

  1. 2. Home = the existing event-gate Cloud Run service. Already deployed and healthy, already

ticks every minute on Cloud Scheduler, already reaches the live panel via alarm-mcp

(https://alarm-mcp-cfnon4wh3q-uc.a.run.app), and already carries the only push path at the desk

that has been proven to page a human (escalation.py, ticket 036, live in ~4 min).

GitHub Actions ruled OUT for this job specifically: the map's own scheduler-reliability fog

records Actions cron silently dropping runs (watchlist 3×/12h). A dropped poll is recoverable;

a dropped deadline check is worse than no check, because it reads as coverage.

  1. 3. The invariant: no booking may start while an Access / PIN flag against it is open.

Re-verify against the live panel, never the row status. A passing panel check auto-closes

the flag silently — no human is touched when the system fixes itself.

  1. 4. The ladder (Junyan's shape — starts the day with a human holding it, then escalates *up the

chain*, rather than staying quiet then bursting):

| Rung | When | Action |

|---|---|---|

| 1 | Morning of the booking | The Doorman tells the morning ops person (Ela) at its existing ~06:00 look — it is already awake, already reads the panel, already raises the row |

| 2 | (re-check loop) | event-gate re-verifies open access flags against the live panel every 30 min; silent while it holds |

| 3 | 2h before start, still broken | Direct message to Junyan — a real page, not a queue row |

| 4 | 45 min before start, still broken | Send the Alarm.com Emergency Code user to the renter + file the mandatory reset row |

  1. 5. Doorman split — half yes. Junyan asked whether this is the Doorman's job. Rung 1 yes;

rungs 2–4 no. The Doorman fires 04:00 + ~06:00 and is then asleep — it did its job perfectly

at 06:02 on Jul 24 and the next 13.5 hours had no owner. Waking an LLM pass all day to watch a

clock is expensive work that plain code already does for free on the gate's 1-min tick.

Doorman spots and tells Ela; event-gate does the nagging.

  1. 6. Rung 4 — the emergency code, with the risk named. The safer design (auto-create a time-boxed

per-renter Alarm.com user, as Junyan did by hand on Jul 24: window 19:36 Jul 24 → Aug 24) is

not available: alarm-mcp is read-only by deliberate design (adc_client.py: *"exposes NO

arm / disarm / lock / unlock calls"*), with no user-creation path. Given read-only, the standing

code is the only tool that gets a renter through a door unattended.

What is being sent is a master key, and the build must treat it as one — the Alarm.com

Emergency Code user is Locations: All / Access Period: At All Times /

Access Type: Existing + Future: every studio, no expiry, including studios not yet added.

Junyan accepted this with the rotation guardrail (next ruling).

The code digits are deliberately not recorded here or anywhere in git — reference the

Alarm.com user by name only.

  1. 7. Mandatory rotation guardrail (Junyan): the moment the code is sent, the system files a row

in ✅ Actions to Perform that the emergency code must be reset — naming recipient, time, studio,

and booking. Plus a loud notice to Junyan and Ela and a J28 ledger entry: a master key leaving the

building is never quiet. Residual risk accepted and banked: if rotation is not actually done,

the guardrail decays into exactly the ignored-queue-row disease this ticket exists to cure —

the reset row must be treated as urgent-until-confirmed, and its rot is the first thing to audit.

  1. 8. All four rungs build together (Junyan, 2026-08-03) — not 1–3 first. Sequenced 1→4 inside one

build ticket so the ladder cannot ship permanently three-quarters done.

Acceptance test (unchanged — replay Jul 24)

Flag raised 06:02, booking 19:30. Under this design: Ela is told at ~06:02; the gate re-verifies

every 30 min all day; Junyan is paged at 17:30 with two hours of runway; at 18:45, if still

broken, Vicky receives the emergency code and a reset row is filed. The 13.5-hour silence is gone.

What this ticket did NOT settle

The other bookend check families in the Question below — existence invariants, reconciliation

invariants, completeness sweeps — were not designed here. They need the cloud checker to exist

first. Graduated as 044, blocked by 043.

Motivating failure — the Vicky Yeung lockout, 2026-07-24 (READ THIS FIRST)

A renter was actually locked out of 509A at 19:30 on Jul 24. This is the concrete cost of

the missing verification layer, and the acceptance test for whatever this ticket builds.

The timeline:

  1. 1. Jul 23, 06:02 — the Doorman's live check returned manual_no_match for Vicky Yeung (no

Alarm.com user at all). It suppressed the alert, reasoning that a prior Access/PIN row

("Extend Vicky's window to cover Jul 24 + Jul 26") was already marked Processed. A full

day of lead time was lost to a Notion row status that did not reflect the panel.

  1. 2. Jul 24, 06:02 — the Doorman got the same manual_no_match, and this time correctly raised

an Action row at Pending Review, explicitly noting the earlier fix had not landed on the panel.

  1. 3. 06:02 → 19:30 (13.5 hours) — the row sat in the queue. Nothing re-checked it, nothing

escalated as the booking approached, nobody actioned it.

  1. 4. 19:30 — Vicky arrived and could not get in. Confirmed by Junyan.
  2. 5. 19:36 — an Alarm.com user was created by hand (window 19:36 Jul 24 → Aug 24). She got in

~6 min late. Jul 26 repeat booking now verifies pass.

Two distinct defects, only one of which is fixed:

  • - (A) FIXED 2026-07-24 in the Doorman prompt — a PRIME DIRECTIVE ("the live panel is the only

truth; a Processed/Done row NEVER suppresses an alert; dedup only against open rows"),

urgent TODAY <HH:MM> — titling for same-day gaps, Raised by = The Doorman (was the retired

Opener), and a second independent access look at 04:00 (step A5) so a broken gate surfaces

~2h earlier with the whole day to fix it.

  • - (B) STILL OPEN — this ticket. *An urgent, correctly-raised flag with a hard deadline sat in a

queue for 13.5 hours and nothing noticed.* Detection was never the problem on Jul 24 — the desk

knew at 06:02. What is missing is the closing half: nothing verifies that a flagged gate

got fixed before the renter arrives. More detection cannot fix this; only an escalating,

deadline-aware check can.

The invariant this ticket must produce (acceptance test — replay Jul 24 against it):

No booking may start while an Access / PIN flag against it is still open. Re-verify against the
live panel (not the row status) on a schedule as the booking approaches; escalate louder as
the clock runs down; and if it is still unresolved close to the start time, put it somewhere a
human is guaranteed to see that hour — not in a queue that is read once a morning.

Open questions for Junyan: which surface escalates (Concierge 2-hourly pass / desk_invariants_v2 /

the Day Sheet's Actions tab), at what thresholds, and who gets pinged when it is 2h out and still

broken — Ops Lead (Ela) by default, but Brazil means it cannot rely on Junyan noticing.

  • - Every re-homed job must report its own result each pass — including zero — and file a

Still open — the escalation half (Junyan's rulings, 2026-07-25)

  • - The re-check lives in the checker program (desk_invariants_v2), not an LLM pass — Junyan's

Question

Re-scoped 2026-07-23 (Junyan, post-007):** the bookends' real purpose was

verification — the Opener's night-watch item count was it checking the overnight

fleet's work, not doing new work. Retiring the runs (007, Ruling 2) was right, but the

verification layer they carried needs a named, durable home. Per the

level-up plan doctrine, the bottleneck is trusted

verification — so the home should be deterministic invariants + monitor-by-exception

(desk_invariants_v2's structured defects → 3-strike escalation to Actions rows), NOT a

resurrected LLM pass that re-reads everything.

Design with Junyan, check by check from the Opener's old Steps 3/4/6/6B/6C/7/7B/8:

  • - Existence invariants (IPR / Overtime / FBS itinerary / watchlist freshness /

Day Sheet freshness): pure date-filtered queries → v2 invariants. The Sentinel covers

run heartbeats; these cover artifact existence — decide if they're one roster or two.

  • - Reconciliation invariants (FBS board ↔ message queue ↔ calendars; duplicate/orphan

board rows; yesterday's hours vs Staff Scheduling): deterministic diffs → v2, with the

Custodian's 09:35 pass as the human-facing reviewer of whatever v2 flags.

  • - Completeness sweeps ([Unpaid] → Money Request Queue incl. prior-day back-check;

GTG no-show surfacing): mostly date math + plan rates — candidate for a small script on

the deterministic lane, with only the discretionary residue routed to Actions rows.

  • - Escalation contract: every failed check must land somewhere a human already looks

(Actions For = Ops Lead / For = Junyan per the 027 split, or the Fixer Report) —

"check exists" is not done until "failure is seen" is wired.

  • - No fire-repair authority (Ruling 2 stands): invariants detect and escalate; the Ops

lead investigates. Nothing auto-refires generators.

Interim coverage until this lands: Custodian 02:35 + 09:35 passes, Sentinel missing-run

detection, desk_invariants v1 hourly. Watch the Fixer Report for rot — unpaid-charge

misses are the most likely first symptom and raise this ticket's urgency.

Coordinate with Phase 0 of the level-up plan (v2 swap is gated on Junyan's explicit go)

— these checks should land as v2 invariants, not a parallel checker.

  • Listed all 13 of its jobs; 7 are already covered, 6 still need a home

  • So a reply is ready in seconds instead of hours, but a human always pushes the send button
  • Also built in guards so nobody ever gets a pile of robot texts: at most 2 automatic replies per person per half-day, and rapid-fire texts get ONE combined reply, not three
  • PROVED FOR REAL Jul 29: Junyan texted "it's too hot in here" and the how-to came back in 90 seconds — no human, no waiting for the hourly robot
  • Same minute, "can I add an hour tonight?" had a written reply waiting for staff — and it was careful: it promised to check, not that the hour was free
  • Two hidden bugs only showed up once real texts came through, and both are fixed — one had been quietly making every fast reply fall back to the slow robot
  • BUILT Jul 31: someone now listens when a renter replies 'still stuck' — an urgent text goes to on-call staff
  • A 'thanks, all good' reply is recognized and closes things out quietly
  • At 901 after dark, a can't-get-in text now calls staff to bring the key — no robot ever mentions key codes
  • Junyan also ruled: only ONE robot writes replies now — the fast one stops composing, ending the two-robots-one-job mixup
  • LIVE Aug 1: all of it is deployed and running (the deploy error was just the wrong Google account signed in)
  • PROVED FOR REAL Aug 1: texted 'what's my code' — got the code back; replied 'still not working' — an urgent alert reached staff in about a minute
  • That's the exact hole that forced us to switch the old robot back on in July
  • The 'thanks, all good' reply stayed quiet too — no false alarm
  • RETIRED Aug 1: the old robot is switched OFF for good. The front desk is two robots now, not three
  • It had sent nothing at all for ten days — the new setup had already taken over the work
  • Big lesson written down: never switch a robot off based on what its instructions SAY it does — check what it actually did
Technical notes

Rehome the Receptionist's jobs, then retire it

Question

Ruled by Junyan 2026-07-25: "We can retire it but we need to find a new place for its

jobs." The 2026-07-24 retirement was reversed the next day because the event-gate

quick-answer lane covered its ⚡ cards but not its other work. This ticket inventories

every job on The Receptionist (lean)

against a home, builds the missing homes, and only then retires the trigger.

Method note (the lesson that created this ticket): the inventory below is taken from

the procedure page AND cross-checked against what the persona actually emitted

(Message Queue rows, Correspondence Log, run reports). The Jul-24 retirement failed

because it was judged against the persona's written scope — whose prompt says

"you never compose reply wording" — while the queue shows it composing

Booking cancellation → move offer (Jul 22). Never retire a persona against its

documented scope alone.

The inventory

| # | Receptionist job (page step) | Covered by | Status |

|---|---|---|---|

| 1 | Inbound sweep, every 15–60 min (Step 2) | event-gate 1-min Phone.com poll | ✅ better |

| 2 | Watchlist gate — who is in the space (Step 3) | event-gate lookup_watchlist | ✅ same source |

| 3 | Card classification, 11 rows (Step 4) | cards.py, 14 cards + wont-lock | ✅ superset |

| 4 | Card auto-send, Mode 2 (Step 5) | queue row → reviewer → send engine | ✅ |

| 5 | Life-safety first-contact summon (Step 6 Case A) | event-gate classifier + actuator | ✅ |

| 6 | Reply-triggered escalation (Step 6 Case B) | — | ❌ GAP 1 |

| 7 | Card B — 901 night lockbox, staff-queued, never auto-send (Step 5.2) | SOP ruling: staff hand off the key in person; no message ever carries the code | ✅ GAP 2 dissolved 2026-07-31 — folded into GAP 1's summon path |

| 8 | Thermostat card → ✅ Actions row for 901 / "can you set it" (Step 4 row 3) | cards.file_actions_row() | ✅ built 2026-07-31 (b79372c) — needs redeploy |
