The gaps, and the proposed home for each

GAP 1 — reply-triggered escalation. The most important, and a LIVE promise breach.

Every card ends "if you need assistance, just reply here and we'll jump in." In the

Receptionist that footer is load-bearing: a still-stuck reply to an EMERGENCY-class card

(901 can't-get-in daytime · 901 can't-get-out · leak · code failing at the door) pages

on-call; a comfort-class one goes to the Concierge. The event-gate lane sends the

same footer and nothing listens. Same defect class as the maintenance-ticket promise:

a card that promises a response must be backed by one.

→ Home: event-gate. It already has the actuator (summons SMS + Staff Call Queue

row) and per-sender state. Needs: remember which card class went to which number today,

classify the next inbound as close-out vs still-stuck, and summon on still-stuck +

emergency-class. Deterministic; no LLM needed.

GAP 2 — Card B, 901 night lockbox. → RESCOPED 2026-07-31 (Junyan), and it got smaller.

Original plan: a hold_for_human card flag that queues the row at Pending Review and

skips the reviewer, so a human releases the 5789 code. That card should not exist.

The lockbox ruling is now explicit in the live SOP

(🔐 Artist is having issues with Door Lock,

Case A + Rules): the lockbox is a staff tool, not a message — a staff member opens the

door with the 527 key and hands it over in person, and no agent may ever draft, queue,

or send the code, to anyone, including the test line. A held draft containing 5789 is still

a message containing 5789; hold_for_human would only have made the wrong thing slower.

→ Correct behaviour: the gate says nothing about keys. A broken-lock report is an

EMERGENCY-class inbound → summon staff (the actuator GAP 1 needs anyway). If a renter asks

for the lockbox code directly, the reply is that a staff member will bring the key.

→ Home: the existing emergency lane + GAP 1's escalation. No new card, no new flag.

This gap closes when GAP 1's summon path exists — it has no independent build left.

GAP 3 — thermostat → Actions row. ✅ BUILT 2026-07-31 (commit b79372c). 901 has no

renter control, and its thermostat how-to says *"I've flagged this to staff so they can

adjust it for you"* — nothing was listening. cards.file_actions_row() now raises the

✅ Actions row (Type=Other, For=Ops Lead, Status=Pending Review, Artist relation set,

Basis quoting the inbound) via the same files_* side-effect pattern as

files_maintenance: at MATCH time, so the promise holds even when the reply is held,

deduped, or the card is unpublished. Gated by files_actions_studios: {"901"} — renters

at the other four studios adjust it themselves and get no row. Dedups against an open

row for that artist inside the 12 h window, and fails closed on an unreadable query

(the inverse of the reply-path dedup: a duplicate "go adjust the thermostat" row wastes

staff attention, and the renter already has their reply either way). Every exit path now

reports its side-effect outcomes via _stamp(), so a suppressed reply still logs whether

the row was raised. 145 tests pass (was 142). Needs a gate redeploy to take effect.

GAP 4 — Gmail thread hygiene. ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-31 (Junyan): "This is covered by the

Concierge not the Receptionist." Owner ruling, no build. JOB 2 keeps sole ownership of

inbox state — the Responder is explicitly barred from labelling, archiving, or marking

read (two writers there would corrupt JOB 2's audit invariants). Nothing to verify and

nothing to rehome: this job was never leaving the Concierge.

GAP 5 — composed / courtesy replies. The Julia cancellation→move offer class.

→ RULED by Junyan 2026-07-28: build it in the gate ("Okay let's build it then"),

after his two-lane latency requirement — emergency replies immediate (already at the

1-min floor), courtesy/ticket drafts "generated as fast as possible… for it to be

usable by staff". 2-hourly Concierge drafting was ruled NOT good enough.

→ BUILT 2026-07-28: drafter.py in event-gate. Two lanes on the gate's own

1-minute clock via direct Messages API calls (claude-opus-5, metered — ~$10/mo at

current volume, deliberately off the subscription):

  1. 1. Inline card review — a freshly queued QA- row is reviewed in ~3 s and flipped

to Ready to Send; card replies land in ~90 s instead of ~11 min. The hourly

GitHub Actions sweep stays on as the safety net for anything held.

  1. 2. Composed drafts — no-card verified messages get a DR- row drafted in Junyan's

voice at Pending Review, never auto-released. Staff open the queue and the

reply is already written (~10 s after the poll sees the text).

→ Barrage guards (Junyan's explicit design constraint — "we don't want a

situation where the person gets a barrage of messages"): per-topic 12 h dedup (was

already there) + per-sender cap of 2 auto-releases/12 h (past it, rows hold for a

human) + fact-preservation check in code (a reviewed body may not introduce any

digit-run or URL the canned original lacked) + one-open-draft-per-renter (a burst of

texts updates the single DR- row — process-local map, durable Notion query as the

cold-start backstop) + pure acks ("ok", "thanks 🙏") never draft.

This satisfies the sibling map's Responder scope for the SMS channel; the sibling

tickets should be annotated rather than double-built.

→ VERIFIED LIVE 2026-07-29 (rev event-gate-00010-slc), both lanes, real SMS:

  • - Card lane — "it's too hot in here" 17:29:13 UTC → qa_inline_review: "released",

thermostat card on the phone inside ~90 s. No sweep involved.

  • - Compose lane — "can I add an hour tonight?" 17:30:14 UTC → dr_drafted: true,

row DR-3887-0729-1330 at Pending Review ~70 s after send, correctly hedged

("let me check the calendar… and get back to you shortly") — no price, no

availability promise, not auto-sent.

  • - Guards observed in live traffic: the 15:18 repeat thermostat text was suppressed

(qa_deduped: true), and the compose burst produced exactly one DR- row.

→ Two build defects found and fixed by this live traffic (both were silent):

  1. 1. review_and_release PATCHed a nonexistent Drafted Body property; Notion voids a

PATCH containing any unknown property, so every inline release failed as

held-notion-error and fell back to the hourly sweep (commit 83a9fd6).

  1. 2. A failed compose returned a bare {"drafted": False} with no reason. Now every

failure names itself (dr_why, held-api-<reason>) — the diagnostic gap that made

the 2026-07-29 04:53 UTC failure unreadable.

→ Residual FIXED 2026-07-29 (commit 52c62d9): the 12 h per-topic dedup was

process-local and reset on deploy (visible at 17:28→17:29 — the pre-deploy revision

suppressed the text, the fresh one sent it). cards.recent_card_row_exists() now asks

the queue DB the same question the in-memory map answers, mirroring the draft lane's

_find_open_draft_row. Fails open on a Notion error — a missed dedup costs one

extra helpful reply; a false suppression costs a renter their answer. Needs a gate

redeploy to take effect.

→ Sweep cadence dropped to hourly (same commit): inline review is now the reviewer

of record, so the GitHub Actions sweep only picks up rows inline review held — rare,

and an hour is fine. The native backstop cron is retimed in qa-review.yml; the

cron-job.org job "qa-review 10-min tick" still needs the same change in its web UI

(that job is what actually fires — the native schedule is only a backstop).

GAP 6 — positive close-out. ✅ CLOSED 2026-07-31 (Junyan): "This also should be owned by

the Responder." "Thanks, all good" is a courtesy/acknowledgement reply carrying no

question or request — already the first line of the Responder's Scope. No build; this

records the owner so it stops reading as unassigned. It does NOT fall to the Concierge

anymore (that path was carved out 2026-07-29).

Retirement precondition (replaces the Jul-24 one)

Retire the trigger when GAPs 1–3 are built and verified, GAP 4 is confirmed, and GAP 5

has a ruled home. Not when the card lane ships.

  • - Running both agents meanwhile is safe — the Receptionist's dedup skips any message

  • - GAP 5 → the last Concierge residue removed. The 2026-07-29 carve-out moved the

composing of the non-ticket reply class to the Responder but left the Concierge

emitting the courtesy-reply approval block in its Output. That moved to the

Responder today: the doer surfaces its own work, and a reviewer can now tell whose

draft they are approving. Concierge Output no longer emits it.

  • - GAP 1 → still open, and the premise that it was covered does not hold. Junyan's

reasoning was that the Responder handles replies and the cards no longer promise a

listener. Both are true, and neither closes this gap. the-responder.md line 26 bars

the Responder from emergencies outright — *"lockout / code-not-working / alarm: never

reply, never auto-send… ticket 006 — NOT built; until it lands, emergencies are

entirely out of your scope"* — and its 30-min cadence is the wrong instrument for a

renter locked out at 21:00 regardless. Split the gap honestly: comfort-class

still-stuck is now covered by the Responder ✅; emergency-class still-stuck is

unowned 🔴. What is missing is not a drafter but a summon — remember which card

class went to which number today, classify the next inbound as close-out vs still-stuck,

and page on still-stuck + emergency-class. Deterministic; no LLM. This remains the sole

retirement blocker.

  • - Also ported into canon this pass: the 527-lockbox key hand-off now lives in the live

🔐 Artist is having issues with Door Lock

SOP (Case A + Rules), where it had previously existed only as tribal knowledge inside a

closed January ticket — the same "one fact, no home" defect this ticket keeps finding.

⚠️ New finding — The Responder and drafter.py overlap on SMS

Both lanes now compose a held draft for a non-ticket SMS inbound from a verified sender.

drafter.py writes DR- rows ~70 s after the poll; The Responder writes its own rows on

a :20/:50 pass with a 3 h lookback. The Responder's dedup check (a) reads the live Message

Queue, so it should skip a DR- row that already exists — but that is dedup by luck of

timing, not by an owned boundary, and two agents doing one job is precisely the

attribution break the probe exists to measure. Evidence it is live, not theoretical:

DR-3887-0729-2044 (event-gate) and The Responder's 21:51 pass both processed the same

2026-07-29 test-line inbound and reached opposite conclusions about whether to draft.

→ RESOLVED 2026-07-31 by owner ruling, recorded in library/the-responder.md: The

Responder is the SOLE composer across ALL channels; the event-gate's composed-draft

lane (drafter.py DR- rows, SMS) is RETIRED. The gate now only sends pre-approved

cards and summons. This picks the opposite branch from the analysis below — the split is

by owner, not by channel+latency — and it is the cleaner rule: one composer, no

attribution ambiguity, nothing to dedup by luck of timing. Known cost, accepted: SMS

composed drafts go from ~70 s to the Responder's ≤30 min cadence. Worth re-visiting only

if a renter-facing complaint traces to that delay. Note the inline CARD review path in

drafter.py is NOT retired — that is the reviewer of record for QA- rows (~3 s) and has

nothing to do with composing.

<details><summary>Superseded analysis (kept for the reasoning, not the conclusion)</summary>

Do not resolve by deleting one lane. They differ in a way that matters: drafter.py

is a ~70 s deterministic-trigger lane on metered Opus (SMS only), The Responder is a

30-min pass with full ledger/calendar/HTR context across SMS and email. The likely

right split is channel + latency (gate owns fast SMS composes; Responder owns email and

anything needing deep context), but that is Junyan's ruling to make, not a build decision.

Owner: this ticket, before retirement — a decommission that leaves two composers running

just moves the ambiguity.

</details>

Build the message-queue send engine — retire the Zapier send Zaps

Question

Execute message-queue-send-engine-spec.md

(2026-07-18, Ready to build): replace the Notion SMS/Email sender Zaps with a stateless

Google Apps Script send engine polling the 📤 Zapier Message Queue, dispatching via

Phone.com (SMS) and Gmail (Email), writing Status + Sent At back.

Why this map: 024 closed with "Zapier retains sending only" — this retires that last

send role. It's also another job moving to the deterministic no-persona lane (Brazil-proof:

cloud-hosted, no Mac, no LLM), and it fixes the Zapier snapshot bug that makes scheduled

sends uncancellable (the queue-fbs skill and staff depend on Will Not Send actually

working).

Non-negotiables (from the spec — do not renegotiate here):

  • - No state between polls: cancellation/edits take effect up to the poll before dispatch.
  • - Claim-before-send write ordering + LockService lock (no double-sends).
  • - Giggster rows skipped silently; failures flip to Error + row comment + alert email;

no auto-retry.

  • - In-thread Gmail replies via Source Email thread URL; broken thread → Error, never a

silent new email.

Build steps (AFK where possible):

  1. 1. Schema prep on the queue DB: add Send After (date+time, America/Toronto) and Status

option Error (spec §3.2). Create the Notion internal integration, share with this DB only.

  1. 2. Write the Apps Script (poll → validate §5.1 → dispatch §5.2/§5.3 → write-back §4/§7),

Script Properties per §8. Confirm the current Phone.com v4 SMS endpoint/payload.

  1. 3. Test against a duplicate test copy of the queue DB with test contacts — run all ten

acceptance tests §9, especially ★5 (cancel a scheduled send) and ★6 (late body edit).

Junyan-hands checklist (HITL):

  • - Apps Script project under the Dance Annex Google account; authorize Gmail scope.
  • - Generate Phone.com API credentials in the control panel; paste secrets into Script

Properties (never chat).

  • - Pause the Zapier send Zaps before pointing the engine at the live DB — production

flip, his confirm; never run both.

  • - Week of parallel-watch, then SOP v4 update (📨 Skill: Send Message, page

3867503281c4812fa58ce17353c34aab) and Zapier plan inventory/downgrade.

Progress (2026-07-20 late night) — #3 exposed a real reply-targeting bug; cutover plan v2

  • - #3 first run = mechanically clean but FAILED the intent, and found a production bug.

Ran against the outbound-only "Your Dance Annex Shifts — Tuesday, July 14" thread

(studio-side hex id from the J28 ledger): row claimed Sent, no error — but the reply

never reached Junyan. Cause: GmailThread.reply() replies to the last message's

sender; on any thread where the studio sent last (very common in production), the

reply boomerangs to the studio itself. Fixed in Code.gs v5: reply targets the

latest inbound message (Session.getEffectiveUser() used as "me"); outbound-only

thread → Error, consistent with never-misdirect. Junyan's pasted browser URL was the

new-style #search/…/FMfcgz… token — unusable by getThreadById; production pointers

come from the desk/ledger as hex ids (#all/<hex>, sometimes #inbox/<hex> — parser

already accepts both), so this is a non-issue for real rows.

  • - Cutover plan v2 (Junyan's ruling): keep the Zapier queue machinery alive ≥2 weeks

as backup + second send path. Since the ghost automation triggers on the "In progress"

status group (= Ready to Send), coexistence requires a trigger split: new status

Send via Zapier, ghost automation + any Ready-to-Send Zaps retargeted to it (not

deleted). Full sequence + double-send boundary rule in SETUP.md "Cutover plan v2".

  • - Ledger poller side-check: healthy (local clone was stale; Action runs green, cadence

~hourly rather than the nominal 30 min — no action needed for this ticket).

Root cause (🔧 Sweep Feedback 38c7503281c4809da6a5f92c77dab107): a rolling report is set Status = In Progress on the day's first pass but nothing flips it to Completed, because the three Front-Desk tasks run at different cadences and none knows it is last — so reports linger In Progress indefinitely. No LLM needed — it's a status flip + a count. (If a richer end-of-day narrative "clean up" is ever wanted beyond the factual roll-up, that rides the Doorman evening pass — the Closer's successor per 007 — but the lingering-In-Progress bug is fixed here, Brazil-proof.)

errored (is at <sha> but expected <sha>) when Gmail re-delivered a message — it tried to CREATE

✅#023❓Replace the paid email-logging service?2026-07-17
  • Question: can our own program save email instead?
  • Answer: yes
Technical notes

Replace the Zapier email-logging Zaps with a deterministic Gmail poller?

Question

Junyan's direction (2026-07-16): Zapier email logging is too expensive and error-prone

(git write-races). Decide whether one deterministic poller (the sms_pull.py pattern,

extended to Gmail) replaces the Gmail-in/Gmail-out logging Zaps as the ledger's writer —

and settle auth, runtime, scope, and whether this moots ticket 015's "move off git" premise.

Frame: HANDOFF-email-ledger-poller-decision.md.

Resolution (2026-07-17)

DECIDED: yes — build the Gmail poller and retire both logging Zaps after a one-day

parallel diff. The six handoff questions, ruled:

  1. 1. Auth: OAuth refresh token via a Google Cloud project, not IMAP app-password.

Rationale: the Gmail API gives stable message ids (gmail-<internal id> — the msg_id

scheme already chosen for 006), reads archived + Sent mail with full headers (fixes

the old "Sent readable but no MIME headers" Zap-path limit — re-verified as an API-path

capability, sent-message-groundtruth-capabilities applies to the Zap path only),

and query/history reads make missed runs self-heal like the SMS poller. App passwords

are simpler but fragile (2FA-dependent, silently revocable) — the wrong trade for an

unattended Brazil-window writer. Secret lives as a GitHub Actions secret (refresh

token + client id/secret), same pattern as the watchlist secrets. Mitigate the one

silent-death risk (revoked/expired refresh token) with the watchlist's loud

401-handling pattern: fail the step with a clear message, never skip-green.

  1. 2. Runtime: GitHub Action, piggybacked on the existing 30-min watchlist workflow in

desk-correspondence — one checkout, one runtime, near-zero marginal Actions minutes,

so the combined budget stays where it is today (~900/mo, comfortably under 2,000).

30-min freshness is acceptable: the ledger is a record, not an alerting path (J03/J29

own urgency). Local-Mac runtime rejected — sleep/skip is the exact failure the Brazil

window can't absorb.

  1. 3. Scope: retire Zap 1 (Gmail-in) and Zap 2 (Gmail-out) — nothing else. Per the 006

specs both are logging-only (trigger → GitHub Create/Update File; no labels, no

downstream). Zaps 3/4 are already dead; Zapier remains for sending (SMS send, message

queue) untouched. Side benefits of retirement: the junk-sender filter, the

no-reply@phone.com double-logging bug, and the Get-File-SHA idempotency workaround all

become moot — the poller excludes junk senders in code and is idempotent by filename.

  1. 4. Completeness: the API poller reads by query/history, so agent-archived mail is

captured (the known in:inbox 2-min race disappears) — a reliability gain beyond cost.

  1. 5. Ticket 015 premise: downgraded. The write-race was concurrency, not git. Post-cutover

the repo has two writers — the correspondence poller and the watchlist canon push — but

the watchlist push is already retry-rebase race-tolerant, and the poller adopts the same

retry-rebase push. Git stays the transport; "move the ledger off git" is now optional

performance work, not an urgent race fix. 015's real remaining content is the consumer

repoint + backfill decisions — its body updated accordingly.

  1. 6. One poller or two: unified ingest, two thin pull scripts (sms_pull.py +

gmail_pull.py) invoked by the one Action job. Same effect as a single

correspondence_pull.py (one cost line, one failure surface) without rewriting the

working, smoke-tested SMS poller. Merge later only if the scripts converge naturally.

Migration path (per the handoff, confirmed): build gmail_pull.py → test on a known

thread both directions → run poller + Zaps in parallel one day → diff ledgers → Junyan

turns the two Zaps off (production flip — his confirm required, junyan-working-style).

Graduated: ticket 024 (build gmail_pull.py + Action piggyback + parallel-diff cutover).

Google Cloud project + OAuth consent + secrets are Junyan-hands steps inside 024's checklist.

Routing — how issues reach Junyan (proposed 2026-07-17)

  1. 1. "Fix it now" — a live wrong state only Junyan can correct (a mis-routed ticket, a wrong held

Actions/For = Junyan. A given mistake files (1) always, and (2) as well when it's a recurring class.

  1. 2. Two-layer split, J29-pattern: LLM = judgment every 2h, writing ticket state; a

after Junyan's cleanups — freshness comes from the renderer, never from firing the LLM more.

  1. 3. One canon, one derived snapshot. The racetrack stays the sole canon (drift is already a

diffs of the snapshot double as a drift detector — evidence for the J01 research (017).

Moving canon off Notion stays with data-sovereignty-wayfinder.

  1. 4. Ops-lead live monitor: the FBS Monitor (https://junyanboon.github.io/fbs-monitor/)

evolves into an at-a-glance studio board for Ops leads fed by the Activity Log,

  1. 5. J01 rearchitecture wanted, not decided here: ledger-fed triage (J28 replaces email

triage) + possible sub-job split (alarm codes / response drafting / prompting Ops lead for

charges). Biggest current pain point: tickets land wrong place / wrong SOP / wrong checkboxes,

hand-cleared via process-studio-tickets. Both SOPs (some legacy) and execution are at

fault — research must attribute per recurring action. Goal: all jobs parked in personas soon

✅#022🚶Walk through a full day together2026-07-16
  • Junyan walked through a whole day of desk work
  • 5 big design choices came out of it
Technical notes

Walk a full Concierge day with Junyan — the lived process, end to end

Question

Junyan's direction (2026-07-16): the 017 evidence pass isn't deep enough — walk through the

WHOLE Concierge process with him across a day's work, so the rearchitecture (018) is designed

from full comprehension of the lived process, not just mined failure classes.

Deliverable: a day-in-the-life process record (markdown asset) capturing, hour by hour /

pass by pass:

  • - what actually arrives (emails, SMS, drops, calendar events, gate flips) and in what rhythm;
  • - what the desk SHOULD do at each point vs what it currently does (tie back to the

017 failure-mode map classes where they surface);

  • - where Junyan's judgment is genuinely needed vs where it's mechanical;
  • - the corrections he makes in real time — the live version of the Sweep Feedback stream;
  • - what "adjusted" looks like to him at each step (his words, not inferred).

HITL by design — Junyan speaks for the process; the walkthrough happens with him live,

ideally over a real day's tickets. Feeds 018 directly.

Resolution — walked live with Junyan 2026-07-16

Full record: 022-walkthrough-record.md (against the

real-day evidence pack 022-today-raw.md).

The relay: night watch (agent preps tomorrow, report → Ops lead) → Ops lead morning

(verify reports → fix agent errors → release messages → clear gates; manual acts →

desk-drops) → agent sweep → afternoon bulk pass (the real volume) → one evening run →

studio-close gate (occupancy zero, not a clock) → night watch. Immediate-support lane

(future duty manager + agent) spans the day. Every agent miss the Ops lead covers gets noted

= the correction stream.

Design rulings produced (the 018 brief):

  1. 1. Unit of work = person/engagement thread, not ticket-per-SOP; SOPs become in-thread

procedures with their own gates (kills the cross-SOP three-ticket confusion).

  1. 2. AI-native store; humans get projections — Day Sheet reduces to the human execute-list

+ customer status report. Racetrack model (positions/gates) survives; the Notion-body

substrate does not.

  1. 3. "Messages are just gates" — one gate model for holds, sends, staff actions.
  2. 4. Persistent per-customer context (past threads + correspondence + about-me profile);

off-system staff exchanges captured; everything queryable.

  1. 5. Per-skill sub-agents — routing ten tools through one agent is where execution drops

things; named: money-sweep sub-agent (sweeps all money sources → packages for the human),

FBS day-assistant agent (chatbot-like, fast, renter-facing, coordinates with the duty

manager via an improved FBS monitor showing queued messages + arrivals/departures +

issues-to-clear → sharpens ticket 020's scope).

Unblocks: 018 (now waits only on 006).

Reliability diagnostic — missed fires vs shallow passes

Question

Pull the Concierge rolling reports and Run Monitor rows since the 2026-07-11 cloud re-migration and separate the two failure modes, which need different fixes: scheduler/thaw misses (run never fired — cross-check the dual-runtime banner arrangement, Claude :10 vs Codex :12) vs density failures (run fired but sub-step counts are missing/zero — quota burned for nothing). Quantify each per day, name the worst sub-steps, and note anything implicating the Receptionist duplicate-report fork (Custodian row 39c7503281c481d3b438d385cc1ec1e2). Output: a findings asset; fixes graduate into their own tickets.

Resolution (2026-07-15)

Findings asset: 003-reliability-findings.md, built from the six Concierge rolling reports + Receptionist/Custodian rows since 07-11.

Headline (missed fires): the authorized cloud runtime [Claude] :10 logged ZERO passes across 07-13 → 07-15. The desk was carried by Codex :12 (07-13 only) and Junyan's manual [Claude-local] kicks (07-14, 07-15) — the 07-15 report says so in its own words. Coverage is human-dependent, which fails against Brazil (Jul 27). Also: Custodian whole-day miss 07-11; within-day hourly gaps on manual days (07-14: ~6 unfired slots).

Density failures (fired-but-shallow): (1) collection_router_upstream_429 shared-SQL throttle aborts JOB 2 dedup + JOB 3 reconciliation mid-pass — the biggest drain; (2) Codex structurally can't read Slack #desk-drops → every Codex pass skips JOB 4; (3) FBS overnight-straggler view object_not_found → JOB 1 straggler skipped; (4) Desk Invariants page intermittently unavailable → JOB 10 inline fallback; (5) a shallow 07-14 pass reported "#desk-drops empty" while missing 5. Worst sub-steps: JOB 2/JOB 3 (429), JOB 4 (Codex capability), JOB 1 straggler (broken view).

Receptionist duplicate fork: confirmed — two Receptionist rows on 07-12 and 07-13, self-resolved to one row from 07-14; largely mooted by ticket 011 (retire the 15-min poll).

Graduated tickets: 012 (restore cloud :10 — the headline; +Codex-drops capability), 013 (SQL-router 429 headroom), 014 (repair FBS straggler view + Invariants page). Added 012 as a blocker of 004 — cadence right-sizing is meaningless while the cloud trigger fires zero passes.

✅#012☁️Turn the cloud robot back on?2026-07-15
  • Found why it was off (a safety flip)
  • Kept off at first; turned back on for the trip
Technical notes

Restore the cloud Concierge (:10) runtime — it is firing zero passes

Question

The reliability findings show the authorized cloud Concierge runtime [Claude] :10 (trig_01XVReGFYaBMcLrDovBMevzp) has logged ZERO passes across 07-13 → 07-15 — the desk is being carried by Codex :12 (07-13 only) and Junyan's manual [Claude-local] kicks (07-14, 07-15). This is a missed-fire defect at the runtime level and it fails hard against the Brazil departure (Jul 27), when manual kicks stop.

Diagnose why the cloud :10 trigger produces no passes, then restore it to fire unattended and verify it logs passes into the rolling report:

  • - Is the trigger disabled, mis-scheduled (UTC-cron / DST — see dance-annex-persona-renames-and-cloud-shifts), or is its report-write step silently failing so it fires but doesn't log? Check the live RemoteTrigger list + the 🚦 dual-runtime production banner (Claude :10 + Codex :12 — dance-annex-persona-renames-and-cloud-shifts).
  • - Confirm the banner actually authorizes :10 to run (the banner, not a task flag, is the gate).
  • - Also fold in the Codex #desk-drops capability gap: Codex structurally cannot read Slack #desk-drops ("unavailable to Codex per live CAPABILITIES"), so every Codex pass skips JOB 4 — a runtime-config gap that belongs with runtime health.

Resolution records: root cause of the zero-fires, the fix (trigger/schedule/banner/report-write edit), the Codex-drops capability decision, and one observed cloud-:10 pass logged unattended into the rolling report.

Resolution — CLOSED 2026-07-15

Root cause (diagnosed via live RemoteTrigger get): the cloud trigger trig_01XVReGFYaBMcLrDovBMevzp is simply enabled: false. It was flipped off on 2026-07-13 ~01:51 UTC (updated_at), ~2 min after its last real fire (last_fired_at: 2026-07-13T01:49:19Z) — consistent with being switched off to avoid double-running while Junyan ran the local runtime by hand. Not a schedule/DST bug and not a silent report-write failure:

  • - cron_expression: "10 0-2,12-23 *" is correct — :10 past the hour, UTC 0-2 + 12-23 = the ~12 Toronto daytime fires (08:10–22:10 Toronto), matching the banner.
  • - It logged passes normally right up until it was disabled; nothing about the report-write path is broken.
  • - Banner check: the 🚦 production banner on The Concierge (updated 2026-07-13) explicitly authorizes three redundant runtimes for the identical workflow — (A) Claude cloud :10 = this trigger, (B) Codex :12, (C) local Mac the-concierge (manual). So the gate authorizes :10; the runtime was merely out of sync with its own banner. Re-enabling would need no banner edit — it's a one-field, fully reversible flip.

Fix: DEFERRED by Junyan's ruling (2026-07-15). Presented the finding + the one-field flip. Junyan ruled keep cloud :10 OFF, rely on local (C) only (chosen over "enable now alongside local" and "enable at a pre-departure cutover"). Fix is therefore fix-ready-but-deferred: whenever enabled:true is wanted, flip it via RemoteTrigger update — no other change required. The ticket did NOT observe an unattended :10 pass (the original success criterion) because the authorized outcome is that :10 does not run.

Codex #desk-drops capability decision (2026-07-15): Junyan chose "Investigate giving Codex Slack read" (over accepting the gap or a manual fallback). Graduated to a new ticket — Can the Codex runtime be granted Slack #desk-drops read?.

Open risk flagged (NOT resolved here): local (C) is manual-kick and its hourly schedule "frequently skips" (Mac sleep + App Nap — local-tasks-miss-runs-on-sleep). Under the local-only ruling, nothing fires unattended during Brazil (dep. Jul 27) once manual kicks stop, and Codex :12 has only ever logged passes on 07-13. This is a genuine unattended-coverage gap left open by the ruling; banked to the map's Not yet specified as the Brazil-window coverage question (Junyan has a plan or will re-decide nearer departure).

Unblocks: Cadence right-sizing (004) — its premise shifts from "right-size a trigger firing zero passes" to "right-size the local runtime's job bundle," but the cadence question stands and is now answerable.

NOT reliably grantable via connector. Junyan's direct experience: the ChatGPT Slack connector cannot read #desk-drops properly; the only working path is Codex reading Slack via its in-app browser. Full findings + corrected implications: 013b-codex-slack-findings.md.

  • - The AFK analysis initially read the Wave-1 shadow (07-11) as proof of connector read; Junyan's ruling overrides — the connector does not work in practice. CAPABILITIES' "UNAVAILABLE" Codex row was substantively correct.
